台灣股市：先進製程帶動結構性擴張，類股良性輪動構築中長線多頭
台灣 4 月外銷訂單金額達 874.5 億美元，創歷年同月新高，年增率高達 48.1%。受到 AI、高效
能運算及雲端資料服務需求的強勁拉動 ， 其中電子產品與資訊通信產品訂單表現尤為顯著 ， 預期未來
數月外銷訂單可望穩定維持在每月 800 億美元的水準。在供應鏈「去中化」趨勢下，全球高端製造
需求正高度向台灣集中 。 半導體持續擴產亦帶動本土精密機械與檢測設備訂單增長 ， 動能已逐步外溢
至傳統產業，使整體經濟走向更全面的復甦。
在外銷訂單高度成長的背後，正是因為台積電在先進製程與封裝技術上確立了絕對領先地位帶動

了整個台灣上下游產業鏈。台積電技術論壇揭示，在 AI 驅動下，2030 年全球半導體市場規模將達
1.5 兆美元，其中 AI 將貢獻 55%以上的營收。目前全球雲端服務供應商（CSP）對輝達 Blackwell
架構的強烈需求，高度仰賴台積電先進封裝產能的釋放。台積電的 2 奈米（N2）製成即將量產，更
先進的製程預計於 2026 年下半年投產，並著手規劃埃米世代製程。反觀英特爾與三星目前仍在先進
製程節點與良率之間掙扎，台積電不僅在技術進程上領先一至兩個世代，更透過良率高達 98%的
CoWoS 先進封裝建立技術壁壘，短期內對手在技術成熟度、量產規模與客戶信任度上皆難以競爭。
基本面的強勁表現反映在台股的籌碼與盤勢結構上 。 隨著5 月 21 日輝達財報化解市場觀望心理 ，
三大法人全面翻多，單日合計買超達 850.51 億元，其中外資單日買超即佔了 603.41 億元，成交值
顯著放大 。 此波修正成功洗淨市場的短線浮額 。 資金從漲多的記憶體板塊撤出 ， 流入基期較低的面板、
載板、 被動元件等產業， 盤面呈現健康且偏多的類股輪動特徵 。 資金不再僅僅獨攬於少數AI 權值股，

而是向各受惠供應鏈上下游擴散，構築了多頭的廣度。
在地緣政治上，5 月川習會整體會談並無重大協議，中美脫鉤的大趨勢持續。中美雙方在台灣問
題上維持各自表述，中方警告「台獨」與台海和平水火不容；美方則重申對台政策維持不變，雙方沒
有重大改變。在全球 CSP 廠商對算力需求尚未觸頂的基本面下，台灣高端製造與半導體供應鏈的核
心地位 ， 並未受到政治會談結果的實質干擾 。 輝達財報行情與訂單表現依然反映出算力稀缺的市場共
識，地緣政治風險僅止於情緒面擾動，未動搖產業趨勢。 台灣在全球 AI 與高效能運算資本支出浪潮
中，憑藉半導體先進製程與封裝技術，展現出極強的定價權與不可替代性。這使得總體經濟指標表現
亮眼，且擴張動能正由科技業外溢至傳統產業。台股呈現健康的類股輪動格局，地緣政治政治雜音並
未動搖高端製造向台灣集中的趨勢，中長線維持看多格局。

拉美市場：巴西總統大選前料將震盪，後市中性看待
5 月以來拉美股市表現相對疲弱，主因隨著 10 月巴西總統大選逐步逼近，選情不確定性升溫，
導致最大經濟體巴西股市出現震盪，同時美伊和談狀況膠著，油價持續維持高檔，帶動整體拉美國家
通膨預期升溫，降息預期縮窄，股市上漲動能便受到壓抑。
回顧歷史，巴西股市於大選前半年走勢普遍不佳，過去五次選舉前六個月MSCI 巴西指數報酬中
位數為負 4%，其中 2014 年與 2022 年兩次大選年更以全年下跌作收，顯示選情雜音升高之際，投
資人多於投票前轉趨觀望。在大選過後，巴西股市報酬率則取決於是否由資本市場偏好的候選人當

選，若是，則股市大多上漲，若否，則股市大多下跌，巴西股市 5 月來的跌幅就與此相關。
本次巴西總統大選中，相對受到資本市場偏好的候選人波索納洛，近期捲入醜聞，導致其支持度
大幅滑落，其被指控與涉案巴西史上最大銀行詐欺案 Banco Master 的前執行長關係密切，並涉及收
受其資金用於拍攝家族紀錄片。受此影響，民調機構 AtlasIntel 5 月 19 日最新民調顯示，在第二輪
對決調查中，魯拉以 48.9%支持度領先波索納洛的 41.8%，波索納洛由原先小幅領先轉為落後約 7
個百分點。由於市場對偏左派的魯拉財政與政策路線疑慮較深，民調再度倒向魯拉，連帶導致巴西股
市表現承壓。
另一方面，受美伊戰爭影響，西德州原油價格持續維持高檔，帶動巴西輸入性通膨壓力上升，市
場對通膨回落的信心隨之轉弱。根據巴西央行截至 5 月 22 日的 Focus 市場調查，市場預期 2026 年
消費者物價指數預期中位數已由四週前的 4.86% 升至 5.04%，進一步突破 4.50%的通膨目標上緣；
受此影響，市場對 2026 年底 Selic 基準利率的預期亦由四週前的 13.00%上修至 13.25%，意味市場

預期下半年降息幅度僅約 1 個百分點，降息預期幅度持續收窄，連帶限制股市評價向上修正的空間。
然而儘管受到選舉及通膨升溫兩股承壓力量，高盛最新報告仍對於巴西股市中長線維持加碼評
等，看多理由在於，巴西股市對本地利率的負相關程度為新興市場中之最，在過去降息循環中，股市
於首次降息後往往出現強勁漲勢，而目前巴西市場仍處於降息循環中；此外，今年以來外資對巴西股
市淨流入居新興市場之冠，亦有利股市支撐。
展望後市，降息循環有利巴西股市支撐，然而考量巴西股市於大選前表現普遍不佳，同時油價高
檔推升通膨預期、降息幅度持續收窄的情況下，指數上檔空間恐受壓抑，故我們對於拉美市場後市目
前抱持中性看法。

能源類股：雖仍供不應求，惟油價料將隨談判進度震盪走低
當前油價與能源類股表現仍取決於美伊和談進展， 5 月 23 日川普宣稱一份結束戰事並重啟荷
莫茲海峽的備忘錄已大致談定，將採先確立大方向原則，後續再就細節分軌協商的模式來結束戰爭，
伊朗官媒指出，荷莫茲海峽通行與核子相關議題，將分別在備忘錄簽署後 30 天及 60 天進行談判，
談判期間美國將分階段豁免伊朗制裁。然而截至最新消息，美伊雙方在荷莫茲海峽主權與通行收費、
鈾濃縮是否完全停止等核心議題上仍存在明顯分歧，故該份備忘錄能否順利簽署仍具不確定性。
受到荷莫茲海峽持續封鎖影響，IEA（國際能源署）5 月月報指出，4 月全球石油供給量再度下

降每日 180 萬桶至每日 9,510 萬桶，下降主因波斯灣國家停產量持續上升，不過美國、巴西、加
拿大等國家持續增加出口，也為全球油市提供部分緩解，IEA 將 2026 年美洲供給成長上修逾每日
60 萬桶至平均每日 150 萬桶，大西洋盆地原油出口也自 2 月以來增加每日 350 萬桶。
此外全球石油需求量下滑，也部分抵銷供給缺口。IEA 將 2026 年全球石油需求下修至年減每
日 42 萬桶，較開戰前的預測減少了每日 130 萬桶，產業面看以石化與航空產業需求下滑幅度最
深，國家別中，中國 4 月海運原油進口量相較 2 月大幅減少每日 360 萬桶，日韓印亦分別縮減每日
190 萬、100 萬與 76 萬桶。全球石油需求下降，主因中東石油供給受阻，導致部分石化公司被迫
減產，此外部分也跟中國經濟表現疲弱，導致的需求下滑有關。
然而儘管全球石油需求量下滑 ， 非中東地區國家也持續增產 ， 全球石油市場仍面臨每日約750 萬
桶的供給缺口，受此影響， IEA 統計全球可見庫存 3 月減少 1.29 億桶，4 月再減 1.17 億桶，

高盛則預估 5 月以來全球可見石油庫存以每日 870 萬桶的創紀錄速度被消耗。
但值得注意的是，儘管全球石油市場面臨嚴重供不應求，理應有利油價上漲，然而整個 5 月西
德州原油價格卻仍維持在每桶 80 至 110 美元區間震盪，並未進一步上漲。高盛指出，油價上漲受
阻，主因經濟合作暨發展組織(OECD)國家以每日 130 萬桶速度釋出戰略石油儲備，填補了部分缺
口，同時中國原油進口量自 2 月 27 日以來大減每日 430 萬桶，使市場上對於可售原油的爭奪趨
緩，連帶使油價漲幅受到受抑。
展望後市油價，EIA（美國能源資訊署）預估布蘭特原油價格 5、6 月均價將約為 106 美元，
隨後續海峽通行恢復，第四季油價可望回落至每桶 89 美元，惟若海峽重開延後至六月底，則後續
油價將可能較此預測在上升每桶 20 美元。整體而言，後市油價及能源類股表現，仍將視美伊談判
進度而定，然而考量美伊戰事持續朝收斂方向前進，預料後續油價將隨談判進度震盪走低，相較不利
能源類股表現，在缺乏進一步利多支撐的狀況下，我們對於能源類股後市目前保守看待。

### Page 9

身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
黃金類股：黃金表現仍承壓，惟全球央行買盤漸強
5 月受到美伊戰爭帶動美債殖利率上行影響，黃金表現仍承壓，然而我們觀察到來自全球央行的
買盤逐漸轉為強勁，顯示各國央行對於逢低增持黃金的興趣仍存，有利為金價帶來支撐，預料來自央
行的買盤將可使金價保持在區間內震盪整理，等待美伊衝突緩解，殖利率上行的壓力消退。
央行買盤方面，高盛於5 月報告大幅上修其央行購金量預估，主因其推估模型自 2025 年 8 月起
系統性低估各國央行購金量，經參數修正後，高盛預估各國央行購金量 12 個月移動平均值由原本估
計的每月 29 公噸調升至每月 50 公噸，單月修正幅度更大，1 月由 12 公噸上修至 66 公噸、2 月由 2

公噸上修至 41 公噸，2026 全年央行購金量則預估為平均每月 60 公噸。
世界黃金協會數據亦呼應此預估，根據世界黃金協會統計 ，2026 年第 1 季全球央行淨購金達 244
公噸，季增 17%，超越前季與五年均值。在已申報買方中，波蘭央行 Q1 增持 31 公噸為最大買家，
烏茲別克央行則增持 25 公噸、哈薩克央行 12 公噸緊隨其後。此外中國人行持續增持黃金動作也未
停歇，在 4 月中國人行單月增持 8 公噸黃金，創下 2024 年 12 月以來單月新高，並且是連續第 18
個月增持黃金儲備，帶動其官方持有量上升至 2,322 公噸，占外匯儲備 9%。從以上數據可以看出，
各國央行在近幾個月金價走疲的階段，仍持續加大力度增持黃金儲備。
在黃金 ETF 資金面亦可見到轉折出現的跡象，繼 3 月黃金 ETF 出現顯著資金流出後，4 月全球
實物黃金 ETF 轉為出現 66 億美元的資金流入，所有地區在均呈正向流入，推動全球黃金 ETF 黃金持
有量從 3 月底約 4,092 公噸反彈 45 公噸至 4,137 公噸，達到歷史第三高 ， 僅略低於2/27 創下的 4,176

公噸紀錄，其中歐洲基金表現最強，4 月流入 37 億美元使年初至今累計由負轉正，主要受地緣政治
風險升溫、英國央行立場不如預期鷹派及本地股市回跌驅動。亞洲則延續第 8 個月淨流入趨勢，4 月
增加 18 億美元，其中香港受惠於新 ETF 產品掛牌上市錄得 7.32 億美元單月歷史新高，中國在地緣
緊張 、 殖利率下行及官方持續宣布增持黃金的背景下亦流入 4.98 億美元。由黃金 ETF 數據亦可看出，
市場資金對於逢低增持黃金配置的興趣正在上升。
展望後市，在國際地緣政治紛擾不斷的狀況下 ，仍可預期央行買盤與個人投資組合中分散風險的
需求可望延續，有利為金價帶來支撐，然而在高油價將可能使利率維持在高檔較久的環境下，黃金
ETF 資金流入恐難以重現 2025 年的強度，故預期中短線上金價將可能持續維持在區間內震盪，惟若
後續美伊談判推展順利，隨荷莫茲海峽開放，油價回落將有利市場降息預期回升，屆時搭配來自央行
的買盤，將有望再度重啟黃金新一輪多頭走勢。

### Page 10

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：市場觀望華許首秀，美債利率料陷整理走勢
自 2/28 美伊戰事爆發並導致荷莫茲海峽封鎖以來 ， 全球金融市場經歷自 1970 年代以來最嚴重
的供給端通膨衝擊。西德州原油與布蘭特原油價格的大幅飆升，引發市場對於通膨，乃至於 Fed 利
率政策方向轉變的疑慮，成為推升美債殖利率的核心驅動引擎。
這場由地緣政治引發的能源危機，並非僅侷限於美國單一國家，而是全球公債市場的普遍現象。
由於全球能源報價以美元計價 ， 且原油實體供給短缺屬於全球性危機 ， 主要已開發國家的公債殖利率
呈現極高相關性的同步上揚 ， 其中又以日本與英國的公債殖利率升幅最大 。 日本 75% 能源依賴中東

供給，不論在物價上或是財政體質上面臨不小壓力。
我們預期 6 月市場的總經焦點將在華許首次主持的 6/17 FOMC 利率會議。4 月下旬在分析華許
在參議院聽證會的發言時 ， 便已提出其主張可能導致 「美債殖利率陡峭化」 與 「債市波動性恐將加大」
的想法。彭博 4/30~5/13 調查顯示，118 名受訪者中有 64% 預期華許上任後，美債利率將出現某
種形式的陡峭化，其中熊市陡峭化（長率升速>短率升速）是主流觀點(佔比近 30%)。未來若債券殖
利率曲線呈現陡峭化(不論是熊市、牛市或是扭轉陡峭化)，短中天期的債券殖利率表現都將相對優於
長天期債券，加上短天期債券的存續期間本就低於長天期公債，因此當債市震盪時，短天期債券價格
表現將優於長天期債券。
巴克萊近期研究也顯示，當聯邦基金利率在 2-5% 的區間內波動，平均約為 3.6% 時，30 年
期公債殖利率與聯邦基金利率之間的利差為 170 個基點。假設長期中性利率在 3-4%的區間內（如
聯準會點陣圖所示），30 年期公債殖利率應在 4.7-5.7%區間。而目前約 5%水準約在區間相對偏低

的水準。巴克萊認為，在美國經濟韌性的背景下，目前30 年期公債殖利率還不算高，考慮到當前的
財政狀況和異常的不確定性，投資人可能需要更高的期限溢酬。若此說法成真，相較於短期債券，長
天期債券殖利率上升幅度更高(價格下跌幅度更大)。
雖然 5 月至今股市有所表現，但在中東地緣政治談判反覆，能源通膨預期以及聯準會偏鷹派立場
的交互牽引下，債券市場表現卻相對震盪，30 年期美債殖利率甚至一度觸及 2007 年以來的高位。
近來市場定價邏輯高度聚焦於「通膨風險與聯準會政策路徑」 ，債券殖利率 6 月仍將持續面臨高檔震
盪的壓力。目前債券殖利率水準明顯高於其歷史均值，我們認為站在息收 (Carry)的角度，債券仍具
長線投資價值。此外，由於預期美債殖利率曲線趨向陡峭化，再加上長天期公債本就有較高的存續期
間，故就債券價格來看，短天期公債/投資級債券的表現料將優於長天期公債/投資級債券，因此我們
仍維持債券投資聚焦中短天期公債/投資級債的建議。

測試業務起家 ，2000 年購入力晶後段設備後開始提供客戶封裝加測試一貫化服務 ，

報價，毛利率因為封測報價繼續調漲將提升，整體展望方向符合預期，獲利預估因

## Document 187: [000270] 기아(000270) 26.3Q Review_ 수익성에 대한 입증이 필요기아(000270) 26.3Q Review_ 수익성에 대한 입증이 필요26.3Q Review_ 수익성에 대한 입증이 필요 - 기업

2026.11.02   
기아 (000270)

26.3Q Review: 수익성에 대한 입증이 필요  Company Brief

26.3Q Review: 매출액 31.6조원, 영업이익 1.8조원  
기아는 26.3Q 매출액 31.6조원 (+10.1% YoY, -4.4% QoQ), 영업이익 1.8조원 
(+24.3% YoY, -30.8% QoQ, +5.8% OPM) 등의 실적을 기록했다. 26.3Q 영업이익은 
시장 컨센서스 (2.36조원)를 22.9% 하회했다 [iM증권 추정치: 매출액 31.5조원, 
영업이익 2.3조원]. 주요 구성 요소별 내용은 아래와 같다. 
① P: 26.3Q 26.3Q 전동화 차량 판매는 30.0만대 (+47% YoY, 판매 비중 
36.2%)를 기록했는데, 이 중 HEV 17.2 만대 (+46% YoY), EV 12.3 만대 
(+75% YoY)로 구성됨. 미국 HEV 판매가 전년 동기 대비 118% 증가하며 
시장 성장률을 약 4배 상회했고, 특히 서유럽 EV 판매가 전년 대비 163% 
증가함. 단 26.3Q 중 원화가 빠르게 강세로 전환되며 원/달러, 원/유로 
평균 환율은 전분기 대비 각 5.2%, 6.2% 하락함에 따른 – 효과 관찰됨. 
② Q: 26.3Q 도매 판매는 84.7만대 (+7.9% YoY) 를 기록했고, 북미, 유럽, 
인도 등 주요 시장에서 고른 성장 나타남. 26.3Q 소매 판매 또한 82.9만대 
(+6.9% YoY)로 글로벌 산업 수요 (-6% YoY)를 상회. 북미는 HEV, 내수와 
유럽은 EV, 인도는 신형 셀토스와 소넷을 중심으로 판매가 확대됨 (이하 
26.3Q 권역별 도매 판매 YoY, 국내: +2.2%, 북미: +7.6%. 유럽: +19.3%. 
인도: +38.5%). 이란 사태 장기화로 아중동 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 
28.4% 감소했으나, 수요가 견조한 중남미와 CIS로 물량을 전환하며 
감소분을 상당 부분 만회함. 연간 도매 판매 가이던스 (335만대)를 유지함. 
③ C: 26.3Q 관세 비용은 약 0.7조원으로 추정되며, 전년 동기 대비 
5,000억원 감소함 (25.2Q 0.7조원, 25.3Q 1.2조원, 25.4Q 1.0조원, 26.1Q 
0.8조원). 반면 인센티브 증가 및 가격 인하 7,270억원, 서유럽 EV 비중 
확대에 따른 믹스 악화 4,650억원, 원자재 가격 상승 영향 3,680억원 등 
총 1.6조원의 감익 요인이 발생함. 이에 26.3Q 매출원가율은 84.1%로 
전년 동기 대비 3.0%p 상승함 (인센티브 +1.9%p, 재료비 +1.2%p). 다만 
26.3Q 기말 환율 급락하며 판매보증충당부채 환입 규모가 축소되었고 
판매보증비 약 9,000억원 감소. 판매관리비율은 10.1%로 3.7%p 개선됨. 
26.3Q 기아 순이익은 1.29조원으로 전년 동기 대비 9.6% 감소했다. 원화 강세에 
따라 외환환산 관련 이익이 전년 동기 540억원 이익에서 3,718억원으로 손실 
전환됐고, 현대모비스 등 관계사 이익 감소로 지분법손익 또한 360억원 감소했기 
때문이다 (현대차 순이익 감소 논리와 동일). 1) 산업 수요 감소와 중동 판매 차질에도 
권역별 파워트레인 대응을 통해 그룹사 대비 가지고 있는 외형 성장성을 증명했으나, 
2) 유럽 EV 판매 확대 과정에서 발생한 인센티브와 믹스 악화라는 비용 부담, 원자재 
가격 상승이 관세 감소 효과를 대부분 반납했고, 3) 분기 중 급격한 원화 강세가 
더해지며 시장 기대를 크게 하회하는 수익성을 기록했다는 것이 26.3Q 실적 총평이다. 
Buy (Maintain)

기아 (000270) 
2

실적 발표 후 주가 급락: 성장에도 불구하고 수익성에 대한 우려  
26.10.30 실적 발표 당일 기아 주가는 11.7% 하락했다. 하루 전 발표된 그룹사 실적 
발표와 상반된 모습이었다. 시장이 이번 실적에서 확인한 우려는 아래와 같다. 첫째, 
성장과 이익의 괴리다. 관세 영향을 제외한 26.3Q 영업이익은 2.52조원으로 전년 
동기(2.70조원) 대비 오히려 1,770억원 감소했다. 판매 증가에 따른 물량 효과 
(+4,420억원)와 환율 효과 (+5,290억원)가 발생했음에도, 인센티브와 믹스 악화로 이를 
대부분 반납한 것이다. 기아의 외형 성장 상당 부분이 유럽 EV 판매 증가에서 나오는데, 
해당 시장에서는 중국 EV 업체와의 경쟁으로 공격적인 프로모션이 불가피하다. 26.3Q 
유럽 권역에서의 중소형 EV 판매가 사업 계획을 크게 웃돌며 EV 믹스는 13.1%에서 
14.8%로 확대됐다. 시장은 이를 M/S를 위해 수익성을 훼손하고 있다고 받아들이는 
것이다. 둘째, 당장 26.4Q에도 유의미한 수익성 개선이 없을 것이라는 점이다. 기아는 
26.3Q 컨퍼런스 콜에서 4Q 영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이며, 연초 
제시한 연간 가이던스 (영업이익 10.2조원, +8.3% OPM) 에 다소 미달할 것이라고 
밝혔다. 중동 분쟁의 영향이 최소 연말까지 이어지고, 원자재와 물류비 부담 또한 
지속될 것이 회사의 판단이다. 무엇보다 원/달러 환율이 26.2Q 평균 약 1,505원에서 
26.3Q 약 1,430원, 현재 1,320~1,340원 수준까지 하락하며 이에 따른 - 영향이 커질 
수 있다. 셋째, 그룹사와의 이익 체력 비교다. 현대차는 26.3Q 파업과 생산 차질로 도매 
판매가 11.3% 감소했음에도 원가 절감을 통해 영업이익이 5.5% 증가했고, 시장 
기대를 상회했다. 반면 기아는 판매가 7.9% 증가했음에도 영업이익이 컨센서스를 20% 
이상 하회했다. 26.3Q 양사 영업이익 격차는 약 8,600억원까지 벌어진 상황이다.  
단기 노이즈를 제외한다면 기아의 기업 가치는 여전히 높다  
그럼에도 당사는 기아의 EV 전략, 낮은 수익성을 이유로 중장기 기업 가치에 대한 
기대를 낮출 이유는 없다고 판단한다. 첫째, 외부 요인을 걷어내면 본원적인 수익 
창출력은 유지되고 있다. 기아는 26.3Q 영업이익률을 낮춘 외부 요인을 원자재 1.2%p, 
중동 판매 차질 0.7%p, 환율 1.7%p 등 총 3.6%p로 분석했다. 즉 중동 분쟁이 
해소되면 상당 부분이 사라질 일회성 요인이다. 둘째, EV 수익성 개선 경로는 HEV가 
걸어온 길과 같다. 기아는 HEV 역시 도입 초기에는 수익을 내지 못했으나 현재는 ICE에 
버금가는 수익성을 내고 있다며, 26~27 년 현재의 EV 전략을 유지하겠다고 밝혔다. 
유럽 ICE 시장은 26.3Q 전년 동기 대비 약 20% 축소된 반면 EV 시장은 50% 이상 
성장하고 있다. 전환기에 스케일을 확보하지 못하면 EV 마진을 개선할 방법도 없다. 
기아는 향후 3년간 배터리, 모터, 히트펌프 등 주요 부품에서 연 10% 수준의 원가 
절감을 추진하고, 2~3년 내 EV를 HEV와 같은 수익성 경로에 올려놓겠다는 계획이다. 
셋째, 중장기 성장 계획을 수정하지 않았다. 기아는 27년 사업 계획이 CID 당시 제시한 
숫자와 큰 차이가 없을 것이며, 중장기 영업이익률 10% 목표도 유지했다. IAA 시행에 
대비해 슬로바키아 공장에서 EV를 전량 생산하고 ICE 국내 수출 방안도 검토 중이다. 
주주환원 측면에서도 TSR 35% 를 지키고, 자사주 매입보다 배당을 우선하겠다는 
입장이다. 10/30 종가와 최소 DPS 기준만으로도 배당수익률은 6.3%에 달하고 있다.

### Page 3

기아 (000270)

그림1. 기아 분기 매출원가 및 매출원가율 추이   그림2. 기아 분기 판매관리비 및 판매관리비율 추이

기아 판매관리비 (좌) 판매관리비율 (우)
  26.4Q 기아 매출액 31.8조원, 영업이익 2.2조원 전망 
기아 26.4Q 실적을 매출액 31.8조원 (+13.2% YoY), 영업이익 2.2조원 (+20.8% YoY, 
+7.0% OPM)으로 전망한다. 26.4Q 판매 성장세는 이어지고, 3Q에 집중 반영된 원자재 
부담 일부 완화와 계절적 성수기 효과로 3Q 대비 수익성은 소폭 개선될 것이다. 다만 
1) 원화 강세에 따른 평균 환율 하락, 2) 유럽 EV 인센티브의 높은 수준 유지 (미국은 
할부 금리 상승으로 QoQ 약 200달러 증가, 유럽은 전분기와 유사), 3) 중동 판매 
차질과 원자재, 물류비 부담 지속을 감안하면 의미 있는 반등은 어렵다. 이는 4Q 
영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이라는 회사의 언급과도 궤를 같이한다. 
기아 목표주가 150,000원으로 하향  
기아 목표주가를 26.4Q 추정치 하향을 반영해 150,000원으로 하향한다. 기아의 판매 
경쟁력은 금번 분기에도 증명됐다. 다만 EV 판매 확대가 수익성 개선으로 연결된다는 
점이 숫자로 확인되기 전까지, 시장은 기아의 성장에 할인율을 적용할 것이다. 중동 
분쟁 해소와 EV 원가 절감이 맞물리는 27년이 기아가 수익성을 증명할 시점이 될 
것이며, 6%를 상회하는 배당수익률이 그때까지 주가의 하방을 지지할 것으로 판단된다.

당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상 
산업추천 투자등급  
시가총액기준 산업별 시장비중대비 보유비중의 변화를 추천하는 것임 
 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

제약업종

◼ 3Q26 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회 
◼ 4Q26 지연 물량 인식 및 신규 공장 기여로 실적 개선 전망 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용). 기
존 공장의 높은 가동률을 기반으로 5공장 매출 기여 확대, 미국 생산거점 
확보, 신규 모달리티 진출을 통한 중장기 성장 지속 전망.

3Q26 Review: 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회  
3Q26 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5% yoy, +12.8% qoq), 영업이익 6,401
억원(+1.1% yoy, +9.1% qoq), 영업이익률 42.9%(-7.5%p yoy)를 기록하며 
당사 추정치 및 시장 컨센서스 상회. 5월 1차 파업에 따른 매출 차질(약 
1,500억원) 에도 불구하고, 기존 1~4공장 Full 가동이 지속되며 견조한 실
적 달성. 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대와 및 미국 록빌 공장 매출 인식도 외
형 성장에 기여. 다만, 신규 생산시설 초기 가동비와 제품 믹스 영향으로 
영업이익률은 전년 동기 대비 하락.

4Q26 매출액 1조 5,764억원(+22.6% yoy), 영업이익 7,075억원
(+34.0% yoy) 전망  
4Q26에는 2분기 파업으로 지연된 생산물량의 순차적인 매출 인식과 미국 
록빌 공장 매출 기여, 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대로 실적 개선 예상, 동사
는 신규 생산시설의 매출 가시성 확보를 바탕으로 2026년 매출 성장률 
20%를 무난히 달성할 것으로 전망(연초 가이던스 15~20%).

신규 수주 회복과 포트폴리오 확장에 주목 
미국 의약품 관세정책 불확실성 완화로 지연됐던 수주 협상이 재개되며 신
규 수주 환경 개선 중. 3Q26 약 3억달러의 신규 수주를 확보했으며, 대형 
계약 협상도 구체화되고 있어 2027년 수주 회복 기대. 이는 5공장 가동률 
확대와 중장기 성장 가시성을 높이는 요인. 6공장은 ‘26년말~’27년초 착
공을 목표로 기존 증설 일정 유지. 
폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 1차 공개매수 응모율 87.88%를 기록하며 연내 
인수 완료 추진 중. 인수 완료 시 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업영역이 확대되며 
글로벌 생산거점 및 고객 기반 다변화 기대.

## Document 266: AMD电话会：Q4 AI芯片收入将首次超越服务器CPU，明年数据中心收入占比将破80%

Source PDF: all report pdf/AMD电话会：Q4 AI芯片收入将首次超越服务器CPU，明年数据中心收入占比将破80%.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

AMD CEO 苏姿丰指出 AI 代理正成为云端 CPU 的 “ 下⼀个杀⼿级应⽤ ” ，且 Q4 数据中⼼ AI 芯
⽚收⼊将历史性地⾸次超越传统服务器 CPU 。尽管预计明年 PC 市场将出现两位数下滑，但
凭借 AI 算⼒基础设施的爆发式需求， AMD 正迎来盈利能⼒和利润率的全⾯扩张。
摘要：
数据中⼼业务爆发式增⻓推动 AMD 业绩全⾯超预期，公司上调明年展望，预计服务器收⼊翻
番、 AI 芯⽚收⼊增速更快。
华尔街⻅闻提及，美东时间 3 ⽇周⼆美股盘后， AMD 公布， 2026 年第三季度营收同⽐增⻓ 45%
⾄ 133.77 亿美元，⼜创单季新⾼，较分析师预期⾼约 2.3% 。⾮ GAAP ⼝径下，每股收益同⽐增
⻓ 74% ⾄ 2.09 美元，较分析师预期⾼逾 6% 。
随后电话会上， AMD ⾸席执⾏官苏姿丰（ Lisa Su ）表⽰，数据中⼼需求正在加速，预计 2027 年
数据中⼼营收将超过此前预期，服务器营收届时将实现翻倍，占总营收⽐例最⾼可达 80%。
她表⽰，公司 " 对未来数年保持卓越营收增⻓的能⼒抱有强烈信⼼，且盈利增速将超过营收增

速 "。苏姿丰在回应分析师提问时坦⾔，当前需求确实⾼于供给，公司正与晶圆、基板、先进封
装、存储及其他关键元器件供应商深度合作以提升产能，并表⽰公司 " 有信⼼ " 凭借与⽣态系统的
深厚合作关系进⼀步扩⼤供给。
在产品战略层⾯， AMD 正同步推进 CPU 与 AI 加速芯⽚两条产品线的更新换代。苏姿丰透露，
EPYC Venice 处理器已于三季度开始出货，⾸批 OEM 系统预计四季度正式上市。
在 AI 加速器⽅⾯， AMD 已启动 MI455X 的批量出货， Anthropic 、 Meta 、 Microsoft 、 OpenAI 和
Oracle 等头部客⼾在三季度完成了⾸批 Helios 机架的部署。苏姿丰称，客⼾正在 Helios 上运⾏最
新模型，整体性能表现令⼈满意，四季度 Helios 的部署规模将⼤幅增加。
此外苏姿丰明确警⽰，预计 2027 年 PC 市场将出现两位数百分⽐的降幅。
彭博援引 Emarketer 分析师 Jacob Bourne 的观点称，此次业绩结果 " 将给投资者带来⿎励，因为

投资者近期正越来越严格地审视 AI ⽀出及其回报 " ，尤其是在科技股和半导体板块经历持续调整
的背景下。
Q3 业绩创纪录， 2027 年总营收预期激增超 80%
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

AMD 在今年第三季度交出了⼀份堪称完美的答卷。当季总营收达到 134 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 45% ；
⾮公认会计准则下（ Non-GAAP ）每股收益（ EPS ）增幅更为惊⼈，同⽐⼤增 74% ⾄ 2.09 美
元。⽑利率也扩⼤⾄ 56% ，同⽐提升了 190 个基点。
真正引爆市场情绪的是管理层给出的远期指引。 AMD 预计第四季度营收将达到约 170 亿美元（同
⽐增⻓约 66% ）。更重要的是，公司对 2027 年给出了极其乐观的预期。
苏姿丰在会上表⽰：
她明确指出，2027 年数据中⼼业务将占到公司总营收的 80% 以上，成为驱动收⼊和盈利增⻓的
绝对主⼒。此外，服务器收⼊预计在 2027 年将实现同⽐翻倍。
在谈及市场最关⼼的供需问题时，苏姿丰直⾔：
历史性交叉： Q4 AI 芯⽚收⼊将超越服务器 CPU
数据中⼼业务是 AMD 本季度的核⼼引擎， Q3 收⼊同⽐暴涨 97% ⾄创纪录的 85 亿美元，占总营收
的⽐例从⼀年前的 47% 跃升⾄ 64% 。
这⼀增⻓背后，是新⼀代产品的全⾯铺开。 MI455X 在三季度开始批量出货，包括 Anthropic 、

Meta 、微软、 OpenAI 和甲⻣⽂在内的领先客⼾已经迅速上线了⾸批 Helios 机架。
对于接下来的第四季度， CFO 胡锦透露了⼀个关键的⾥程碑数据：
为了进⼀步加码 AI 军备竞赛， AMD 在三季度宣布了两项关键收购（ Taalas 和 World Labs ）。其
中， World Labs 的联合创始⼈兼 CEO 李⻜⻜将作为⾸席科学家加⼊ AMD ，领导模型研究并帮助
塑造未来的软硬件系统路线图。
“AI 代理正成为云端 CPU 的下⼀个杀⼿级应⽤ ”
在深度⻓篇的电话会中，苏姿丰多次提及⼀个核⼼词汇 ——Agentic Workloads （ AI 代理⼯作负
载），并将其视为重塑计算基础设施的核⼼⼒量。
苏姿丰在分享⾏业洞察时指出：
基于⽬前的可⻅度，我们现在预计 2027 年的营收将增⻓超过 80%，这是我们去年 11 ⽉在财务分
析师⽇上预期的⻓期财务模型的两倍多。
需求确实⾼于供应，因此我们继续与供应链合作伙伴合作以增加供应。鉴于我们在整个⽣态系统
中拥有的强⼤关系，我相信我们有很好的机会做到这⼀点。

第四季度数据中⼼业务环⽐将实现约 50% 的激增。实际上，这将是数据中⼼ AI 业务（ GPU 等）
收⼊超越服务器业务（ CPU ）的第⼀个季度。相较于服务器端，数据中⼼ AI 业务在 Q4 将出现⼀
个⾮常显著的阶跃式上升。

### Page 3

凭借在性能和总拥有成本（ TCO ）上的优势， EPYC 处理器正在疯狂抢占市场份额。苏姿丰特别
提及：
此外， AMD 最近推出的 Helion 获得了前所未有的早期需求， OEM 和 ODM 合作伙伴承诺提供的
Venice 平台数量⽐上⼀代多出 20% 。
隐忧与对策： PC 市场明年⾯临两位数衰退
在 AI 业务⼀路⾼歌猛进的同时， AMD 的客⼾端和游戏业务则⾯临着宏观环境的逆⻛。
第三季度，客⼾端收⼊同⽐增⻓ 7% ⾄ 2.9 亿美元，但环⽐下降了 4% 。苏姿丰对 PC 市场给出了直
⽩的预警：
⾯对 PC 市场的寒冬， AMD 选择避开低端市场的价格战。苏姿丰表⽰：
不过， PC 市场的疲软并未影响公司的整体盈利扩张逻辑。 CFO 胡锦强调，随着营收在 2027 年激
增超 80% ，公司的运营⽀出增速将不到营收增速的⼀半，这将带来 “ ⾮常显著的营业利润率扩张
和盈利能⼒提升 ” 。
AMD Q3 业绩电话全⽂（ AI 辅助翻译）：
云提供商在本季度增加了⼤量 EPYC （霄⻰）容量，⽤于个⼈和企业代理，这些代理正迅速成为

云端 CPU 的下⼀个杀⼿级应⽤。这些代理在应⽤程序和企业系统中连续并⾏地⼯作，在增加加
速器需求的同时，创造了对 CPU 需求的急剧增加。采⽤才刚刚开始，我们预计随着代理被更⼴
泛地部署并承担更复杂的⼯作，需求将急剧增⻓。
OpenAI 选择了 Turin EPYC 处理器作为其⾸个定制加速器平台的主机 CPU 。
本季度末 PC 终端售出放缓，因为较⾼的内存和组件成本给需求带来了压⼒。我们在 2027 年的规
划假设是， PC 市场的单位出货量将出现两位数百分⽐的下降。
我们将专注于能够增加最⼤价值的细分市场，如企业级、⾼端消费级和台式机渠道。我们认识到
低端市场存在⼤量波动，坦率地说，这也是我们敞⼝最⼩的部分。
主持⼈：  欢迎参加 AMD 2026 年第三季度业绩电话会议。⽬前所有参会者处于仅收听模式，正
式演⽰结束后将进⾏问答环节。请注意，本次会议正在录⾳。
现在，我将把会议移交给财务战略与投资者关系副总裁 Matt Ramsey 。 Matt ，请开始。
Matt Ramsay ，企业副总裁，财务战略与投资者关系：

感谢主持⼈，欢迎参加 AMD 2026 年第三季度财务业绩电话会议。

### Page 4

相信各位已有机会查阅我们的业绩新闻稿及随附幻灯⽚。如尚未查阅，可在 AMD.com 的投资者
关系⻚⾯获取相关材料。
今天的电话会议中，我们将主要参考⾮ GAAP 财务指标。完整的⾮ GAAP 与 GAAP 对账表已包含
在今天发布的新闻稿及⽹站上发布的幻灯⽚中。
今天的与会嘉宾包括：董事⻓兼⾸席执⾏官 Lisa Su 博⼠，以及执⾏副总裁、⾸席财务官兼财务
主管 Jean Hu 。
本次为实时直播，并将通过⽹络研讨会在我们的⽹站上进⾏回放。在正式开始之前，请允许我说
明， AMD 本季度将参与以下⾦融界活动： 12 ⽉ 1 ⽇（周⼆）的美国科技与 AI 会议，以及 12 ⽉ 9 ⽇
（周三）的巴克莱第 24 届全球科技年会。
今天讨论中包含的前瞻性陈述，是基于我们当前的信念、假设和预期作出的，包括有关财务预
测、业务和⾏业趋势的前瞻性陈述，仅代表截⾄今⽇的判断，并涉及⻛险和不确定性，可能导致
实际结果与当前预期存在重⼤差异。
请参阅新闻稿中的免责声明，以获取可能导致实际结果产⽣重⼤差异的相关因素说明。

现在，我将把发⾔权交给 Lisa 。
Lisa Su ，董事⻓兼⾸席执⾏官：
感谢 Matt ，也向今天收听的各位致以问候。第三季度是我们有史以来规模最⼤、盈利最强的季
度，这得益于我们开始推进下⼀代数据中⼼产品的量产。营收同⽐增⻓ 45% ，达到 134 亿美元，
创下纪录，主要驱动因素来⾃ EPYC 和 Instinct 的创纪录销售以及嵌⼊式业务的强劲增⻓。每股收
益增速更快，增⻓ 74% ，达到 2.09 美元。
本季度我们执⾏表现出⾊： Helios 量产按计划推进， EPYC 在云端、企业及超级智能⼯作负载中
的采⽤持续扩⼤。数据中⼼业务收⼊的环⽐增速在今年每个季度均持续加快，该业务板块在第三
季度贡献了总营收的 64% ，⽽⼀年前这⼀⽐例仅为 47% 。
我们预计这⼀加速态势将在第四季度延续，主要驱动⼒来⾃推理及智能体⼯作负载需求的增加，
以及最新产品的加速量产。
数据中⼼业务
数据中⼼业务收⼊同⽐增⻓ 97% ，达到创纪录的 85 亿美元。在服务器领域，云端和企业销售均
实现同⽐翻番以上。我们⼤幅超越了此前 " 增⻓逾 80%" 的业绩预期，实现了显著的收⼊份额提

升。
客⼾将 EPYC 部署到更多应⽤场景，智能体⼯作负载贡献了显著增⻓。在云端，超⼤规模云⼚商
在本季度部署了⼤量 EPYC 服务器，⽤于运⾏更多内部服务并扩展其公有云产品。 Turin ⽬前是公
有云中部署最⼴泛的 EPYC 代际产品，来⾃各⼤主要⼚商的 500 余个实例类型已⾯向全球开放。
云⼚商在本季度还新增了⼤量 EPYC 容量，⽤于⽀持个⼈和企业智能体 —— 这正迅速成为云端
CPU 的下⼀个 " 杀⼿级应⽤ " 。
Meta 的 Muse 和 OpenAI 的 Dots 展⽰了个⼈智能体的快速增⻓势头 ——Muse 智能体在上线前六周
内激活⽤⼾已超 1000 万。企业客⼾同样⾏动迅速，正在软件开发、供应链和 IT 运营等领域积极
部署智能体。这些智能体持续并⾏运⾏于各类应⽤和企业系统之中，在推动加速芯⽚需求增⻓的
同时，也显著带动了 CPU 需求的提升。⽬前相关部署仍处于起步阶段，随着智能体的更⼴泛部
署和承担更复杂任务，我们预期需求将⼤幅增⻓。

### Page 5

在头节点领域，我们在本季度赢得了多家顶尖模型开发商和云服务商的重要部署合同，这些合同
将在未来⼏个季度陆续落地。 EPYC 领先的性能表现和成熟的⽣态系统，已经在⼤规模⽣产环境
中得到充分验证，使其成为定制加速平台⾸选的主机 CPU 。以 OpenAI 为例，其⾸批⾃研加速器
平台即选⽤了 Turin EPYC 处理器作为主机 CPU 。
在企业市场， EPYC 服务器渠道销量创下历史纪录，来⾃新⽼客⼾的需求均⼗分强劲。本季度，
我们新增了数⼗家⾸次采⽤ EPYC 的客⼾，涵盖⼤型⾦融服务、通信、零售和医疗健康企业。现
有客⼾同样在将 EPYC 确⽴为其主要计算平台，从特定⼯作负载的部署拓展⾄更⼤⽐例的整体基
础设施覆盖。
在第三季度，我们已开始向客⼾发货 EPYC Venice 处理器，⾸批 OEM 系统也将于本季度正式上
市。从初期需求来看， Venice 是迄今为⽌我们在新⼀代 EPYC 上看到的最强劲反响。 OEM 和
ODM 合作伙伴已确认超过 240 个 Venice 平台，⽐ Turin 多出 20% ；云⼚商也已于本季度开始部署

Venice ，相关实例计划于 2027 年初正式推出。
我们近期发布了 Helion—— ⼀套基于 Venice 的参考设计，可为智能体基础设施提供交钥匙式
EPYC 机架级系统。客⼾可针对各⾃⼯作负载对 Helion 进⾏定制：从为超⼤规模云⼚商提供全
CPU 机架，到企业级的 Venice 与 Instinct GPU 混合系统 —— 后者可同时运⾏智能体和模型。在全
CPU 配置下，单台 Helion 机架可搭载多达 144 颗 Venice 处理器，拥有近 74,000 个线程，实现业界
最⾼的智能体计算密度。各⼤主要服务器 OEM 和 ODM 均计划提供 Helion 产品，⾸批系统将于本
季度出货。
放眼更宏观的市场，服务器计算需求正在云端、企业、智能体及超级智能基础设施领域全⾯加
速。 EPYC 覆盖所有这些市场，拥有业界最宽泛的产品组合 —— 针对云端和智能体的单路性能领
先，针对企业和头节点的单核性能领先，以及针对不同⼯作负载优化的内存与 IO 配置选项。
EPYC 在此基础上进⼀步以显著更优的性能功耗⽐、性能核⼼⽐和性能价格⽐领跑所有其他 x86

及 ARM 服务器 CPU 。随着 TAM （总可寻址市场）预计到 2030 年增⻓⾄约 2200 亿美元，这将使我
们有能⼒持续扩⼤市场份额并显著壮⼤服务器业务。
数据中⼼ AI 业务
前沿实验室、超⼤规模云⼚商和云服务商扩⼤了 Instinct 在推理和训练领域的部署，推动营收创
历史新⾼。
本季度我们开始⼤批量出货 MI455X ，包括 Anthropic 、 Meta 、微软、 OpenAI 和 Oracle 在内的核
⼼客⼾已迅速完成了⾸批 Helios 机架的上线部署。客⼾⽬前正在 Helios 上运⾏其最新模型和⼯作
负载，初期部署表现出⾊。
随着产能持续提升，以及 HPE 、戴尔、联想、超微等 OEM 伙伴陆续开始出货系统，我们预计
Helios 的部署量将在第四季度⼤幅增加。 MI455X 在计算算⼒、内存容量和横向扩展带宽⽅⾯均
处于⾏业领先地位，⽽ Helios 上市后，其在内存带宽和纵向扩展带宽⽅⾯相较竞争对⼿的优势将
进⼀步拉⼤。
我们正与制造和 OEM 合作伙伴密切协作，随客⼾扩⼤部署规模同步扩充产能。

在超级计算领域， EuroHPC 选择 Instinct MI430X GPU 和第六代 EPYC 处理器为 LUMI-AI 提供算
⼒⽀持 ——LUMI-AI 是欧洲计划于 2027 年下半年上线的下⼀代旗舰超算。 MI430X 的双精度计算
性能接近竞品的九倍，这对科学模拟⾄关重要。 MI430X ⽬前已被选⽤于全球多台新建超算系
统，随着各国政府和科研机构规划新⼀轮重⼤投资，我们也处于有利位置赢取更多订单。
在软件⽅⾯，我们发布了 ROCm 10 ，针对⼤规模⽣产部署带来重⼤升级，包括⼤型 GPU 集群的
加速启动以及针对⻓时间运⾏⼯作负载的更⾼韧性。 ROCm 10 还新增了 ROCm.ai 智能体性能调
优功能，可对⼯作负载进⾏分析，识别瓶颈，并⾃动应⽤和验证优化⽅案，将以往需要数周的⼯

程优化压缩⾄数⼩时。客⼾也在利⽤⾃⼰的 AI 智能体在我们的开放软件栈上对模型进⾏优化。例
如， Anthropic 曾分享过⼀个案例：⼀名⼯程师利⽤ Claude ，在单个周末内即完成了最新模型在

我们与前沿实验室的合作，已实现了让其模型协助处理 Instinct 的复杂软件开发与性能调优⼯

本季度，我们宣布了两项收购，以强化超级智能产品组合。 Taalas 拥有经验丰富的⼯程团队和专
为低延迟⾼效推理设计的专⽤芯⽚，我们计划将其技术整合⾄我们的解决⽅案组合，以满⾜低延

有世界⼀流的研究团队，专注于空间智能及对物理世界建⽴认知的模型研究，将帮助我们深⼊洞
察前沿模型和⼯作负载的演进⽅向。李⻜⻜将以⾸席科学家⾝份加⼊ AMD ，负责领导我们的模
型研究⼯作，并参与塑造未来的硬件、软件和系统路线图。预计两项收购均将于年底前完成。

展望与前瞻
放眼未来，我们的核⼼任务是⼤规模落地执⾏。 Venice 、 MI450 系列和 Helios 的量产均处于早期
阶段，三条产品线均有望继续⼤幅增⻓。
⽬前，我们预计数据中⼼业务收⼊增速将在第四季度进⼀步加快，全年业务收⼊将较 2025 年实
现翻番以上。我们对 2027 年的前景判断也在持续增强。
上季度，我们曾表⽰预计年度服务器收⼊增⻓超过 70% ，数据中⼼业务全年收⼊实现翻番。此
后，客⼾扩⼤了 EPYC 和 Instinct 的部署计划，我们也进⼀步落实了更多供货资源以⽀撑增⻓。⽬
前，我们预计 2027 年服务器收⼊将实现同⽐翻番，数据中⼼ AI 收⼊的增速也将超过 90 天前的预
期。因此，我们预计数据中⼼业务在 2027 年将占到总营收的 80% 以上，并成为营收和盈利增⻓
的主要驱动⼒。
放眼更⻓远， MI500 的客⼾接洽持续进展顺利，市场兴趣极为浓厚，因为这⼜是⼀次性能上的重
⼤跨越。我们正与多家前沿实验室开展深度合作，针对其⼯作负载对 MI500 进⾏优化，这些客⼾

正在规划多吉⽡规模的部署⽅案。
客⼾与游戏业务
客⼾与游戏业务收⼊同⽐下降 8% ，⾄ 37 亿美元。其中客⼾业务收⼊同⽐增⻓ 7% ，⾄ 29 亿美
元，主要受 Ryzen 移动处理器强劲销售拉动。商⽤ PC 正成为我们客⼾业务中越来越重要的组成
部分，⼤型企业正在全体员⼯中推⼴部署 Ryzen Pro 商⽤ PC 。全球⼗⼤银⾏中有 8 家、全球⼗⼤
医疗健康和汽⻋企业中有 7 家，⽬前均已采⽤ Ryzen Pro 商⽤ PC 。 OEM ⼚商持续扩展基于 AMD
芯⽚的商⽤产品线，从⽇常商务 PC 到⾼端⼯作站，惠普、联想等⼚商已推出搭载最新 Ryzen AI
Max Pro 400 系列处理器的商⽤笔记本电脑和⼩尺⼨台式机，这些系统可实现⼯作站级性能，并
能在本地运⾏参数量⾼达 3000 亿的超级智能模型。
从市场整体来看，受内存和组件成本上升拖累需求， PC 销售在季末有所放缓。我们正在相应调
整业务节奏，使进货量与销售量保持⼀致，以保持客⼾和渠道库存的健康⽔平。在 2027 年的规

划中，我们预计 PC 市场出货量将出现两位数百分⽐的下滑。在此环境下，我们将凭借持续迭代
的产品路线图和不断扩⼤的商⽤市场份额来跑赢⼤盘。
游戏业务收⼊同⽐下降 40% ，⾄ 7.73 亿美元，主要受控制台周期当前阶段半定制芯⽚销售下滑影
响，符合预期。游戏显卡收⼊同⽐下滑，但 Radeon GPU 渠道销量环⽐有所增⻓。在游戏业务以
外， Radeon AI Pro 收⼊较上季度⼤幅增⻓，越来越多的开发者选择 AMD 在本地运⾏和微调模
型。受单位性能价格⽐领先优势及针对最新开源模型优化的开放软件栈驱动，我们预计 Radeon
AI Pro 将延续强劲增⻓势头。
嵌⼊式业务

### Page 7

嵌⼊式业务收⼊同⽐增⻓ 29% ，⾄ 11 亿美元，实现连续第四个季度的加速增⻓。各终端市场需
求⼗分强劲，以航空航天与国防、汽⻋、通信和⼯业客⼾的增⻓最为突出。数据中⼼建设的持续
推进也在控制平⾯和光学⽹络应⽤中拉动了对嵌⼊式 CPU 和⾃适应 SoC 的更多需求。
我们继续推进 2026 年超过 180 亿美元设计订单的落地⽬标，这将是我们连续第三个创纪录年。⾃
收购赛灵思以来，⾃适应设计订单每年均实现增⻓，嵌⼊式 x86 和半定制订单的增速更快。随着
这些订单在未来⼏年陆续量产，我们预计嵌⼊式业务将实现强劲的两位数年增⻓，并持续跑赢市
场。
总结与展望
综上所述，我们交出了⼀份优异的第三季度成绩单，并预计随着数据中⼼和嵌⼊式业务增⻓对低
迷客⼾ CPU 销售的充分对冲，第四季度将更为强劲。
我们以有史以来最强的产品组合和显著的客⼾动能进⼊ 2027 年。我们已联动整个供应链，锁定
了⽀撑明年更⾼增⻓所需的晶圆、基板、先进封装、内存及其他关键组件资源。
基于当前的业务可⻅度，我们预计 2027 年营收增⻓将超过 80% ，这是我们去年 11 ⽉分析师⽇上

所披露的⻓期财务模型⽬标值的两倍以上。数据中⼼业务将是最⼤贡献者，驱动⼒来⾃ Helios 部
署的持续扩⼤以及 EPYC 市场份额的持续提升。我们同样预计嵌⼊式业务明年将加速增⻓，受益
于⾃适应架构及 x86 解决⽅案的⼴泛采⽤。
展望 2027 年以后，我们正在加⼤硅、软件和系统路线图的研发投⼊，并与最⼤客⼾紧密协作，
共同⽀撑他们未来三⾄五年的宏观基础设施规划。我们也在深化与供应链合作伙伴的战略协作，
并与他们共同投资，以锁定⽀撑我们⻓期增⻓所需的产能。
综合来看，这⼀切让我们对未来数年持续实现卓越营收增⻓、盈利增速更快的⽬标充满信⼼。

下⾯我将先回顾本季度财务业绩，再为各位提供 2026 财年第四季度的业绩展望。
第三季度财务业绩回顾
第三季度业绩表现出⾊，营收达到创纪录的 134 亿美元，超出指引区间上限，主要受数据中⼼业

务板块驱动 —— 该板块收⼊同⽐近乎翻番。
营收环⽐增⻓ 16% ，主要受数据中⼼和嵌⼊式业务板块强劲增⻓拉动，但被客⼾和游戏业务板块
的下滑部分抵消。数据中⼼业务板块约占总营收的 64% ，较上季度的 58% 进⼀步提升，彰显了我
们业务结构的持续优化及其对 AMD 增⻓贡献的不断增强。
⽑利率为 56% ，同⽐提升 190 个基点，反映出更优化的产品结构。运营⽀出为 36 亿美元，同⽐增
⻓ 29% ，主要因我们持续加⼤研发投⼊，以扩展数据中⼼硅、系统及软件能⼒。运营利润同⽐增
⻓ 75% ，达到创纪录的 39 亿美元，增速⼤幅超越营收增速。运营利润率为 29% ，同⽐提升逾 5 个
百分点，彰显了业务规模扩⼤带来的运营杠杆效应。
第三季度，我们开始按会计规则确认此前公告的客⼾协议项下所发⾏认股权证的相关会计影响
—— 该影响于我们开始出货 MI450 系列 GPU 时产⽣。我们的 GAAP 摊薄每股收益为 1.46 美元，其
中包含约 11 亿美元的⾮现⾦⽀出（计⼊其他收⼊与⽀出项），主要反映公司股价上涨及认股权证

触发条件满⾜概率估算值变化的影响。

### Page 8

未来，随着我们和客⼾在该协议下持续推进，这⼀季度性⾮现⾦公允价值调整预计将有所增加，
每季度的具体⾦额将因 AMD 股价及其他估值假设的变化⽽有所不同。这些⾮现⾦调整不影响⾮
GAAP 业绩及⾃由现⾦流。
我们的⾮ GAAP 摊薄每股收益为 2.09 美元。
各业务板块情况
数据中⼼板块：  收⼊达到创纪录的 85 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 97% ，主要受第五代 EPYC 处理器强劲
需求拉动服务器收⼊创纪录，以及 AMD Instinct MI450 系列 GPU 发布带动数据中⼼ AI 收⼊创纪
录。环⽐来看，数据中⼼收⼊增⻓ 27% ，主要由 MI450 系列初期销售及强劲的服务器收⼊增⻓驱
动。数据中⼼板块运营利润为 29 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 168% ，运营利润率由⼀年前的 25% 提升⾄
34% ，彰显出数据中⼼业务的强⼤运营杠杆效应。
客⼾与游戏板块：  收⼊为 37 亿美元，同⽐下降 8% ，主因半定制及显卡销售下滑，部分被最新⼀
代 Ryzen 移动处理器需求增⻓所抵消。其中客⼾业务收⼊ 29 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 7% ，受 Ryzen 移

动处理器销量提升驱动；环⽐来看，受内存成本上升拖累 PC 出货量，客⼾业务收⼊下降 4% ，但
⾼端处理器产品结构改善形成了⼀定对冲。游戏业务收⼊ 7.73 亿美元，同⽐下降 40% ，主因半定
制收⼊下降及显卡需求疲软；环⽐来看，游戏收⼊基本持平。客⼾与游戏板块运营利润为 5.73 亿
美元，占板块收⼊的 15% ；去年同期为 8.67 亿美元，占⽐ 21% 。
嵌⼊式板块：  收⼊为 11 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 29% ，环⽐增⻓ 14% ，多个终端市场需求增强，航空
航天与国防、汽⻋、通信及⼯业终端市场增⻓最为突出。嵌⼊式板块运营利润 4.53 亿美元，占板
块收⼊的 41% ；去年同期为 2.83 亿美元，占⽐ 33% 。
资产负债表与现⾦流
本季度，库存环⽐增加约 30 亿美元⾄ 115 亿美元，以满⾜强劲的数据中⼼需求。经营活动产⽣的
现⾦流量为 12 亿美元，⾃由现⾦流为 5.54 亿美元，反映出营运资本投⼊增加，以及为⽀撑数据
中⼼业务快速增⻓⽽进⾏的持续资本⽀出，包括专⻔服务数据中⼼需求的后端产能投资。我们预

计将继续加⼤库存和产能投⼊，以⽀撑 2027 年的强劲增⻓。季末现⾦及现⾦等价物和短期投资
合计 158 亿美元，总债务 71 亿美元，其中包括今年 8 ⽉完成的 47.5 亿美元优先票据发⾏。
2026 年第四季度业绩展望
预计营收约为 170 亿美元，上下浮动 3% 。指引中值代表同⽐营收增速约为 66% ，主要由数据中
⼼业务板块的加速增⻓驱动。环⽐来看，预计营收将增⻓约 27% ，其中数据中⼼业务板块将实现
约 50% 的⼤幅环⽐增⻓，客⼾与游戏业务板块预计环⽐下降逾 20% ，嵌⼊式业务板块预计实现两
位数增⻓。
此外，第四季度⾮ GAAP ⽑利率预计约为 55% ，⾮ GAAP 运营⽀出预计约为 37.5 亿美元，⾮
GAAP 净利息及其他⽀出预计为约 400 万美元的净收益，⾮ GAAP 有效税率预计为 13% ，摊薄股
份数预计约为 16.7 亿股（已包含 Taalas 和 World Labs 收购预计于第四季度完成的估算影响），第
四季度股份数量预计不受客⼾认股权证的影响。

结语
第三季度是 AMD 的卓越季度，营收创历史纪录，运营利润率显著提升，盈利实现⼤幅增⻓，彰
显了公司整体的严谨执⾏⼒。随着数据中⼼业务规模持续扩⼤，我们的盈利能⼒和⻓期股东价值
将得到充分提升。

Tom O'Malley ：  晚上好，感谢接受提问，恭喜取得优异成绩。 Lisa ，我想从您刚才对 2027 年的
前瞻指引说起。营收增速为 80% ，但数据中⼼业务增速更快，明年数据中⼼将占到总营收的 80%
以上。能否请您就供给侧情况进⾏说明 —— 包括晶圆、封装、基板、内存等 —— 以及从需求侧来
看，如果能获得更多供给，需求是否会超过供给？
Lisa Su ，董事⻓兼⾸席执⾏官：  当然， Tom ，感谢你的问题。进⼊第四季度和 2027 年，我们
拥有很强的增⻓动能，整体需求旺盛，涵盖数据中⼼ CPU 业务和数据中⼼ AI 业务。为应对这⼀
增⻓速度，我们已为此规划了数年。我们与客⼾进⾏了⼤量深⼊沟通，充分了解其部署需求和计
划节奏；同时也与供应链合作伙伴密切协作，确保晶圆、基板、先进封装、内存等所有关键组件
到位。
需求确实⾼于供给，因此我们仍在持续推进与供应链合作伙伴的扩产⼯作，凭借整个⽣态圈中我
们所具有的深厚合作关系，我认为这是完全可以实现的。从⽬前的位置来看，能够对外表⽰整个

公司增⻓超过 80% 、尤其是服务器业务将在 2026 年已较⾼基数上实现翻番、⽽数据中⼼ AI 业务
可⻅度和需求依然强劲，这令我们对于 2027 年数据中⼼业务的发展感到⼗分乐观。
Tom O'Malley：  ⾮常有帮助。再问⼀个关于客⼾业务的后续问题：您提到第四季度将环⽐下降
逾 20% ，这与市场上其他⼚商的反馈基本⼀致。但 2027 年的情况值得关注 —— 通常第⼀季度具
有季节性下⾏规律，复苏往往要等到第⼆、三季度。请问这轮调整预计持续多久？为什么 AMD
此前能够有所超越？以及 2027 年如何⾛出低⾕？
Lisa Su ，董事⻓兼⾸席执⾏官：  PC 市场对我们来说依然是⼀个重要市场。我们在过去⼏次财
报电话会议上⼀直在提⽰，预计下半年将呈现淡季表现，实际情况确实如此。当零部件价格上涨
时，整机定价也随之提升，消费端市场对此⾃然更为敏感，销量因此有所放缓。我们正审慎管理
业务，将进货量与终端销量保持⼀致，确保渠道和客⼾库存保持健康。
我们对 2027 年的规划假设是 PC 市场出货量将出现两位数百分⽐下滑。我们会重点聚焦于能发挥

最⼤价值的细分市场 —— 如企业市场（我们⽬前仍有提升空间）、⾼端消费、桌⾯渠道，这些都
是我们的优势阵地。
好在我们的供应链在 PC 业务和数据中⼼业务之间具有较强的灵活调配能⼒，这使我们能够根据
市场信号灵活调整供应。整体⽽⾔，我们⽬前依然处于需求⼤于供给的状态，因此会继续努⼒在
未来⼏个季度增加供给。

在 2028 年及以后，我的核⼼观点是：我们正处于强劲的整体增⻓轨道之中，拥有坚实的业务基

们的出发点是：在投资增⻓的这⼀⾸要原则指导下，有策略地、有选择性地推进此类合作。

Jean Hu ，执⾏副总裁、⾸席财务官兼财务主管：  Josh ，感谢提问。在运营⽀出⽅⾯，正如你

杆表现令我们相当满意 —— 收⼊增速⼤幅超越运营⽀出增速，推动运营利润率提升。从第四季度

Lisa 提到，我们预计整个公司增⻓超过 80% ，运营⽀出的增速应低于营收增速，且应不超过营收
增速的⼀半，这将推动 2027 年运营利润率实现⼤幅提升。

⽑利率⽅⾯，我们对整体情况相当满意。第三季度，我们正在推进 MI450 的产能爬坡，数据中⼼
AI 业务的表现实际上超出了我们进⼊季度时的预期。第四季度，数据中⼼业务将环⽐增⻓ 50% ，
⽽这正是数据中⼼ AI 业务超越服务器业务的季度 —— 众所周知， AI 业务对公司整体⽑利率有⼀定
的稀释效应，但⽑利润总额正在⼤幅增⻓。
展望 2027 年，数据中⼼业务将占到公司总收⼊的 80% 以上，业务结构的变化将对公司⽑利率产
⽣重⼤影响。数据中⼼ AI 业务将在全年持续放量，服务器业务预计实现翻番，不同季度之间的业
务结构差异会导致⽑利率出现⼀定波动。嵌⼊式业务在 2027 年的增强，也将有助于提升整体⽑
利率。

### Page 11

综合来看，我们的⽬标是实现强劲的营收增⻓和⽑利润增⻓，同时在运营⽀出增速低于营收增速
的驱动下，显著扩⼤运营利润率和整体盈利能⼒。

Jean Hu ，执⾏副总裁、⾸席财务官兼财务主管：  感谢提问。简单来说，认股权证包含三个部

按授予⽇公允价值相应扣除了认股权证的收⼊减免部分，⼤家⽆需单独处理。

摊薄股份数 —— 即需要完成指定出货量并满⾜若⼲商业条件。待进⼊ 2027 年后，我们将就预计
达到归属和⾏权条件的时间节点提供明确指引，届时会有充分的透明度。
第三部分是季度性⾮现⾦公允价值重估⽀出，不影响⾮ GAAP 业绩及⾃由现⾦流。股份数量⽅
⾯，请等待我们进⼊ 2027 年后的明确指引。

第七问： Joseph Moore （摩根⼠丹利）
Joseph Moore ：  感谢。我们听到了很多关于 Helion 机架的积极反馈。在全 CPU 配置下，这⼀

Lisa Su ，董事⻓兼⾸席执⾏官：  Joe ， Helion 机架确实引发了相当热烈的积极反响。核⼼出发
点在于，这类智能体机架需要根据所在环境进⾏深度优化。在超⼤规模云场景下，全 CPU 配

置、⾼密度部署是主流， Venice 作为单路性能最优的处理器，在满配全 CPU 的情况下可实现极
⾼的智能体计算密度。在企业场景下，则会出现 CPU 与 GPU 混合部署的需求，让⽤⼾能够真正
根据⼯作负载灵活优化配置。 Helion 已向开放计算项⽬（ OCP ）贡献了全套开放规范，完全开
放的机架设计也赢得了市场的积极评价。来⾃ OEM 、 ODM 和最终客⼾的反馈均⾮常正⾯。
Joseph Moore：  好的，感谢。再问⼀个问题：服务器实现 100% 增⻓的情况下，贵公司是否认

Lisa Su ，董事⻓兼⾸席执⾏官：  Joe ，实现这样的增⻓本⾝意味着我们已满⾜了⼤量需求。服
务器 TAM 未来三四年的年均增速⼤约在 50% 左右，增⻓来源于三个领域：传统云端和企业、智
能体 CPU ⼯作负载和头节点。我们相信，在给出的约 100% 增⻓预测中，每个细分市场都在实现
份额提升。
我确实认为市场上还有更多需求尚待满⾜，我们正积极与供应链合作伙伴共同解决这⼀问题，同

时也要确保套⽚、 DDR5 等配套组件准备就绪。需求来源⾼度多元化，⼏乎覆盖了每⼀个正在扩
⼤ AMD 部署规模的客⼾，这是令⼈振奋的积极信号。我们会持续努⼒增加供给，满⾜这些不断
落实的增量需求。

Portfolio Considerations
We view recent volatility as a rolling
correction rather than the end of
the cycle. We favor diversified Equity

exposure, with potential opportunities
extending beyond Growth to Value,
Small- and Mid-caps, Non-U.S. Equities
(especially Emerging Markets), dividend-
focused and thematic strategies.
We favor adjusting portfolios toward
strategic durations given attractive
elevated nominal and real yields, and see
value in high-quality bonds, particularly
longer-dated municipal bonds.
Consider broader portfolio
diversification with potential
opportunities across industrial metals,

energy, Real/Tangible Assets, Hedge
Funds, private markets and global
strategies to capture themes from AI,
reindustrialization, infrastructure growth
and market dispersion.

MACRO STRATEGY
The Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran:  Bessent’s Three-Front War and the Stakes for Investors
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury Secretary, but these days he looks more like
America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches from Tokyo, where he is trying
to engineer a stronger Japan yen; to the U.S. Treasury market, where he is battling to keep

the 10-year yield from breaking decisively above 5%; and Tehran, where he has become the
point man in the escalating U.S. campaign to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Make no mistake about it: Secretary Bessent and the Treasury Department are increasingly
an instrument of American economic statecraft, charged not simply with financing the
government and overseeing the financial system, but with managing markets, currencies
and geopolitical adversaries simultaneously. Welcome to the new age of financial warfare.

Front One: Defending the Yen. The first front unfolded over the summer when Secretary
Bessent and the BoJ stealthily intervened in support of the yen, which had fallen to a near
40-year low against the dollar by late July. To arrest the yen’s decline, the U.S. Treasury sold
euros instead of dollars in support of the yen, while pushing the BoJ to make use of the
Fed’s emergency repo facilities. In further support of the yen, the BoJ did what Bessent and

the markets have been pining for, raising its official rate by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%.
Since intervening in the foreign exchange markets, the yen has appreciated by nearly 5%
against the U.S. dollar, alleviating concerns of a disorderly unwind of the yen and giving
Bessent—thus far—an important victory. Defending the yen, of course, isn’t simply about
Japan—it’s about the U.S. and the need for foreign capital inflows.
Japan is one of America’s largest foreign creditors. A persistently weak yen creates

pressure on Tokyo to defend its currency, potentially by selling some of its enormous
holdings of foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys. That prospect didn’t sit well with the
Treasury secretary. At precisely the moment Washington needs foreign capital to finance
its enormous fiscal deficits, the last thing Mr. Bessent wants is one of America’s largest
creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities. Hence, the battle to backstop

Japan’s currency.
Front Two: Defending the 10-Year Bond. Mr. Bessent has had less success on the
second front: the U.S. bond market. While the 10-year Treasury yield slipped slightly
following last week’s decision by the Fed to boost the fed funds rate by 25 bps, the
benchmark interest rate is up roughly 85 bps since the start of the year. Stubbornly high
inflation, elevated oil prices, enormous government borrowing requirements and growing
investor anxiety over America’s fiscal trajectory have all converged and conspired to keep

the 10-year yield closer to 5% than 4%.
Field Marshal Bessent is very aware that the 10-year Treasury is the gravitational center of
global finance—and that mortgage rates, corporate borrowing costs, equity valuations and
the government’s own interest bill all take their cue from it. A sustained move materially
above 5% would tighten financial conditions across the American economy, potentially
threaten the Equity bull market, and pile more pressure on the Republican Party.

To prevent this from happening, the Treasury has expanded its bond-buyback operations,
including purchases of longer-dated government securities, in an effort to improve liquidity
and relieve some pressure on the market, while shifting new government debt issuance
into short-term bills. Yet the bond vigilantes have so far proved harder to intimidate than
currency traders. Even after Treasury announced additional purchases, yields continued
climbing before receiving some relief from the latest hawkish move from the Fed.

Over the medium term, if investors believe Washington is borrowing too much, inflation is
running too hot or the supply of bonds is overwhelming global demand, they will demand a
higher yield to own America’s debt. This battle is far from over.
Front Three: Squeezing Tehran. Then there is Iran, where Mr. Bessent is wielding an
entirely different weapon: America’s control over the plumbing of global finance. In practice
Investment Implications
We expect continued investor

support and preference for real hard
assets, and a bias towards a plethora
of commodities and industrial plays.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
this means the Treasury can make a foreign bank choose between doing business
with Iran or the U.S. It can freeze assets, blacklist companies, restrict transactions and
pressure allies to close financial escape routes.
Under “Operation Economic Outcast,” billed as economic “D-Day,” Treasury has
intensified sanctions against Iranian entities and financial networks, including recent

actions against Iran aviation and additional efforts aimed at banks and organizations
facilitating Tehran’s international transactions. Mr. Bessent has described the objective
in unusually stark terms: to “economically asphyxiate” the Iran regime. This is economic
warfare in its purest form, but whether it works remains to be seen.
“Asphyxiating” an economy takes time and patience—and international cooperation
and coordination. It means closing multiple global loopholes of third-party entities still

willing and able to do business with Iran—look no further than Russian banks. In addition,
squeezing a major oil producer runs the risk of aggravating global oil supplies, keeping
oil prices elevated. Higher energy costs, in turn, feed right back into the U.S. inflation
outlook the Fed is trying to tame and keeps the bond vigilantes on guard and ready to
push back. Not unlike the battle to suppress U.S. bond yields, the battle to bring Iran to
heel is going to be a long one.

How does it all end? How Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A
decisive victory on all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets
is next to impossible, while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best-laid plans.
That said, however, the U.S. Treasury Secretary has signaled that he is ready to take
a very proactive, pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less
encouraging news stems from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the

Treasury secretary.
Mr. Bessent can pressure Iran because access to the dollar-based financial system
remains extraordinarily valuable. He can intervene in foreign-exchange markets because
America’s financial resources give Washington enormous clout. And he can attempt to
influence the Treasury market because U.S. government securities remain the foundation
of the global financial system. But therein lies the contradiction.
The U.S. is simultaneously weaponizing the financial system abroad while becoming more

dependent on that same financial system at home. Washington wants the world to fear
losing access to dollars while also asking the world to lend it trillions of dollars. It wants to
use Treasury and dollar dominance as instruments of geopolitical coercion while running
fiscal deficits that require foreigners to continue believing deeply in those very assets.
The more Washington borrows, the more sensitive it becomes to bond-market discipline.
The more aggressively it weaponizes access to the dollar, the greater the incentive for

adversaries to seek alternatives. And the more the Treasury secretary intervenes directly
in markets, the more investors may begin wondering where economic policy ends and
market management begins.
Exhibit 1: Scott Bessent's Three-Front War.
The Front The Problem Bessent's Objective The Weapon
The Yen Weak yen/currency instability Stronger yen Foreign Exchange intervention, policy coordination, pressure on Japan
The Yield Rising 10-year Treasury yield Lower/contained yields Debt-management strategy, buybacks, issuance policy

Iran Tehran's access to global finance Maximum financial pressure Sanctions, asset restrictions, dollar-system leverage
Source: Chief Investment Office.
For the moment, Mr. Bessent is fighting on three fronts. He wants a stronger yen, a
lower 10-year Treasury yield and an economically isolated Iran. Success on any one front
would be difficult. Achieving all three simultaneously will require considerable skill—and
perhaps a little luck.

MARKET VIEW
Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”
Anna Potts, CFA, Vice President and Investment Strategist
Since the launch of ChatGPT, investors have celebrated AI’s potential while growing
more concerned about its resource footprint: Behind every AI-generated response is
a physical network of data centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems,

semiconductor facilities and critical-mineral supply chains. No doubt, the AI revolution
has environmental costs. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power, water,
advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and intensify
pressure on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend beyond
AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
AI’s footprint is large, growing and certainly not trivial. AI increases electricity demand,

adds large, concentrated loads to already constrained grids, and may slow the retirement
of polluting energy assets. U.S. data centers could account for 20% of national electricity
consumption by 2035, up from 5.9% today, with power needs reaching 194 gigawatts
(comparable to the electricity demand of more than 150 million homes). 1 Cooling, power
generation and semiconductor manufacturing require water; each new megawatt also
embeds substantial copper, aluminum and other materials; and short hardware cycles

can increase electronic waste. Use varies sharply by workload: Complex reasoning and
agentic tasks can consume many times more energy than a simple text prompt. The
effects are already reaching consumers and communities. Wholesale power costs on
PJM—the largest U.S. grid—rose 76% year-over-year in Q1 2026, while data-center
load included in its previous two capacity auctions added an estimated $13.8 billion to
customer bills. 2 Communities are mobilizing around their concerns, including affordability,

water use and air pollution: Q1 2026 produced the largest single quarterly concentration
of delayed or blocked data-center projects on record. 3 Opposition is therefore becoming
a development constraint to be considered.
Context, caveats and comparisons. Headlines often tell only part of the story. Globally,
data centers account for about 1% of electricity use, 4 modest relative to many industries,
and 9% of estimated global 2025 to 2030 electricity growth. 5 A typical text prompt on

one major company’s AI model 6 uses 0.24 watt-hours of electricity, emits 0.03 grams
of CO₂ and consumes 0.26 milliliters of water—roughly 617,000 prompts match the
emissions from producing and cooking one sirloin steak, while 262,000 match the water
used in an eight-minute shower. 7 Still, billions of interactions, more complex workloads
and growth in resource-constrained regions can create meaningful cumulative impacts.
The most meaningful assessment therefore depends on workload, utilization, location,

grid mix, cooling design, local water availability and who bears the cost of supporting
infrastructure, not one universal number.
AI’s Handprint. While much remains to be seen, AI’s environmental “handprint”—the
benefits it can enable—could potentially prove larger than its direct footprint in some
areas. Across the energy system, AI can help utilities better match electricity supply
and demand, balance grids, optimize storage and reduce wasted energy. Data centers

already account for more than 30% of corporate renewable power-purchase agreements,
helping create long-dated demand for clean generation. 8 Beyond power, AI can improve
precision agriculture, streamline transportation and inventories, and support product
1 BloombergNEF , Data Centers on Track to Suck Up a Fifth of US Power Use by 2035, July 21, 2026.
2 BloombergNEF , AI Buildout Drives 76% Power Bill Jump on Largest US Grid, May 14, 2026.

Investment Implications
Responsible AI growth requires
investment in power generation,

grid modernization, energy storage,
advanced cooling, water treatment
and reuse, efficient computing, and
materials recovery and recycling.
Diversified environmental and thematic
strategies may offer exposure to
these physical enablers while reducing
reliance on benchmark names.

design, predictive maintenance, waste sorting and recycling. AI is transforming climate
risk management by using satellite imagery, weather data and predictive analytics to
forecast floods, wildfires and extreme weather events, enabling governments, companies,
and investors to anticipate risks and allocate capital more effectively (Exhibit 2). These
gains are not automatic: they depend on innovation, adoption, and whether efficiency

improvements reduce total resource use rather than simply stimulate more demand. All
industries are faced with the opportunity to responsibly use AI to make operations more
efficient, and much is yet to be discovered.

AI’s potential handprint becomes more tangible in transportation, agriculture and climate
resilience. In aviation, for example, AI can combine weather forecasts, flight paths and
atmospheric conditions to reroute aircraft around regions where warming contrails
are most likely to form—reducing climate impact without requiring a new aircraft or

fuel system. On farms, machine learning can interpret aerial imagery and field-level
sensor data to identify crop stress early, directing water, fertilizer and chemicals to
where they are needed and helping growers improve yields while reducing inputs. AI
can also strengthen climate preparedness by analyzing satellite imagery, weather data
and infrastructure exposure to detect deforestation, anticipate floods or wildfires,
and identify communities, facilities and supply chains most vulnerable to physical

hazards. These are just a few examples that illustrate how AI’s value can extend beyond
incremental efficiency gains to reshape real-world decisions.
Investment Considerations. The challenges created by AI also create potential
investment opportunities. Companies are investing in efficient chips, closed-loop cooling,
reclaimed-water systems, energy storage, grid upgrades and cleaner firm power to
reduce resource intensity and improve reliability. Professionally managed environmental

and thematic strategies can target these “picks and shovels” of the digital buildout,
alongside businesses using AI to improve efficiency across energy management,
water infrastructure, environmental services, waste management and compute. A
disciplined thematic approach can diversify exposure beyond the largest benchmark
constituents while positioning for structural growth in power demand, grid modernization
and resource efficiency. Focusing solely on AI’s footprint risks overlooking both the

companies helping it scale more efficiently and the broader environmental benefits its
handprint may enable.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The Beauty of September
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Call it an eventful first 13 trading days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per
barrel, diesel prices surpassed $6 per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S.
Treasury yield breached 5%, 9 and the Fed joined the ECB and the BoJ in hiking rates for

the first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has shed 1.4% since the start
of the month, while its equal-weighted counterpart is down by 3.3%.
It’s not all too unexpected that September would be a choppy month for U.S. Equities.
Historical seasonal patterns suggest as much. Since 1928, the average S&P 500 return
in September has come out to -1.1%, 10 not only the worst month of the year on average
but also the only month when the benchmark has ended lower more often than it’s

ended higher. Similar weakness has been seen in midterm election years, with the S&P
500 producing an average September return of -0.8% since 1950. 10
The good news is that this has historically been an attractive entry point for U.S. Equity
investors. Call it the beauty of September: In contrast to September’s average decline,
average returns improve in October (+3.0%), November (+2.7%), and December (+0.8%).
Indeed, the S&P 500 has never been negative in the six or 12 months post-midterm

election since 1950 (Exhibit 4A), boasting an impressive average return of 13.8% and
15.8% over those respective periods. 10
Looking ahead to the coming weeks, we have little reason to believe that 2026
will diverge from the historical trend. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election, in line with what’s typical in the months surrounding the
midterms (Exhibit 4B). A quieter corporate calendar ahead of Q3 earnings means

investor focus will remain on incoming macro data, the path of energy prices, and the
Fed’s policy path.
In the meantime, while the S&P 500 still stands just 2.7% below its mid-August high,
churn beneath the surface already appears to be taking place. Just 38% of S&P 500
members now stand above their 50-day moving average versus 70% a mere month ago. 9
Far from worrisome, a correction in the near term in the context of a strong fundamental
economic backdrop would be a potential buying opportunity in our view.

## Document 294: Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

Central banks: a pause while waiting for more information

As expected, the Fed, the ECB, and the BoJ left their rates unchanged this week. This pause , however, does not mark 
the end of the cycle. The ECB’s tone is becoming more balanced, weighing inflationary risks against tightening financial 
conditions, but one final rate hike in December remains our base case. Across the Atlantic, Kevin Warsh describes an 
environment conducive to further tightening, while remaining cautious about the risk of a credit shock. In Japan, the 
lasting exit from the deflationary cycle is taking shape, and monetary policy adjustment is expected to continue 
gradually. We anticipate two rate hikes by the Fed by January and four by the BoJ by the second half of 2028.

Eurozone: A more balanced tone, but the course remains unchanged. The ECB’s statement marks a shift from September. 
It now explicitly acknowledges both upside risks to inflation (the magnitude and persistence of the energy shock) and downsid e 
pressures (tightening financial conditions). The statement that inflation would  remain “well above target for an extended period” 
no longer appears. In essence, however, there is little change. A rate hike in December remains our base case scenario, widel y 
anticipated and implicitly signaled by Christine Lagarde during the Q&A (“If the current situation is not called into question by the 
next set of economic data, the path is clear”). Lagarde presented the rise in sovereign yields as a global phenomenon that is  
tightening financial conditions; regarding the TPI, she stuck to the usual stance: the ECB will intervene if monetary policy 
transmission were to be hampered “by disorderly movements not justified by fundamentals” . By December, the indirect and 
second-round effects of the energy shock will be closely scrutini sed. From our perspective, September’s producer prices offer 
clearer signals, but the increase remains limited to a few sectors: energy, electricity-intensive industries and electrical equipment, 
driven by IA 1. For now, companies in the Eurozone, particularly in the services sector, appear to be absorbing the shock. The 
European Commission’s survey released this Thursday indicates a rise in the sales price index in October, which remains in line 
with recent months.

United States: The Fed navigates between risks. The FOMC pauses after September’s rate hike and maintains the federal 
funds target at 3.75%-4.0%. However, Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh outlined a landscape conducive to further tightening: stronger 
growth, “rather positive” labor market conditions, and, above all, inflation that is “too high.” As a result, two dissenting votes (out 
of 12) were cast in favor of a 25bps hike.  But the committee also appears to fear the adverse consequences of tightening too 
quickly, which is why it is waiting to “accumulate more information.” AI is increasingly at the heart of monetary policymaking. In 
particular, K. Warsh warned against the risks of an abrupt tightening of interest rate conditions, including for the AI investment 
cycle - which triggered a steepening of the yield curve. He also announced that he would soon (on November 16) attend a hearing 
of the “Productivity and Employment” task force to “refine his views” on the implications of AI for the dual mandate. Governor Lisa 
Cook, who voted for a 25bps hike, had noted ahead of the committee meeting “ongoing inflationary pressures” and a risk that they 
might not “subside quickly”. In addition, K. Warsh announced that there would be fewer meetings resulting in rate decisions: 7 in 
2027 and 6 in 2028, compared with 8 today. The change is expected to take effect starting in the fall of 2027. The meetings freed 
up by this change would allow the committee to focus on “strategic deliberations” and long-term issues.

Japan: A status quo, but a tightening cycle set to continue as the economy emerges from deflation. The Bank of Japan 
decided, in line with our forecasts and the consensus, to keep its policy rate target steady at 1.25% at its October 29-30 meeting. 
One dissenting vote called for a rate hike (25bps), contrasting with the two dovish dissenting votes (opposed to a rate hike) at the 
previous meeting. As in the US, t he decision is justified by the need to further analy se risks to economic activity, prices, and 
financial conditions. Above all, the central bank’s outlook for economic activity and prices marks a turning point: it explicitly states 
that “core inflation has reached a level generally in line with price stability” and that it is “expected to remain there.” Governor Ueda 
refined this by noting that it is now necessary to “anchor” inflation at this level before declaring the target achieved. Furthermore, 
the emphasis on “upside risks to prices” and the fact that an y rise in core inflation is now deemed “undesirable” indicate that 
monetary tightening will continue.

The monetary tightening cycle continues. The next steps are likely to be more significant in the United States, where inflation 
has exceeded the target for nearly six years and coexists with a favorable outlook for growth and employment. In the Eurozone, 
the rise in inflation is primarily due to a supply shock in energy costs (though it is also fueled by demand in the United St ates). It 
continues to have little impact on measures of core inflation, including those tracked by the ECB, such as the H ICP; and 
households’ long-term inflation expectations remain anchored. The degree of monetary tightening also differs. In real terms, the 
ECB’s current rates (the deposit facility rate deflated by the core CPI) hover around 0%, a level that more or less corresponds to 
estimates of the natural rate. The Fed, meanwhile, remains in accommodative territory: the real rate (Fed Funds deflated by Core

1 This is a point underscored by the first PPI releases for September (Spain, Italy).

### Page 2

PCE) is at around 0.8%, 40bps below the committee’s implied long-term level (1.2%). Japan remains an outlier: the rise in rates 
preceded the inflationary shock and, until now, has been more a matter of normalization. It is therefore more extensive and spread 
out over time. The central bank is adjusting to the new inflation regime and remains cautious in its exit from accommodative 
policies. The starting point is significantly lower, with real rates still negative, but the adjustment cycle should ultimately bring it to 
the upper end of the neutral range estimated by the BoJ (1.1% -2.5%). We therefore anticipate one final rate hike by the ECB in 
December, two more hikes by the Fed, first in December and then in January, and four spaced-out hikes by the BoJ through the 
second half of 2028.

Anis Bensaidani   
Economist, United States, Japan

## Document 334: Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

14 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.
SOURCE: S&P GLOBAL, BNP PARIBAS

These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.

## Document 336: First Impressions_ NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change of OCR call (2026-11-04)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change of OCR call (2026-11-04).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change
of OCR call
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:19 October 22 2026 Share
Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter and 3.7% y/y. Core inflation
pressures are elevated and market pricing is primed for a 25bp OCR hike next week, which
we now expect.
Consumers Price Index, September quarter 2026 – key stats

### Page 2

Headline inflation
Quarterly change: +0.7% (prev: +1.5%)
Westpac forecast: +0.7%

Westpac forecast: +1.3%

Tradables
Quarterly change: +0.2% (prev: +2.7%)
Westpac forecast: -0.2%
Annual change: +4.3% (prev: +4.9%)

Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter. That saw the annual inflation rate easing to
3.7%, down from 4.1% in the year to June. 
Today’s result was in line with our forecast, but lower than the RBNZ’s forecast for 3.9% annual
inflation. 
However, under the surface, the details were more concerning. Measure of core inflation have taken a
step higher, with several closely watched measures now at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s
target band. 
We suspect that today’s result will leave the market continuing to price a strong chance of another
OCR hike at next Wednesday’s policy review. In that light, we think the RBNZ will regard this as the
path of least resistance, and we now expect that they will deliver (previously we expected that they
would pause and assess, before hiking at the December MPS).

What contributed to inflation in the September quarter?
Underpinning September’s 0.7% rise in consumer prices were some big price movements in both
directions. 
The largest move in prices this quarter was actually a sharp fall in fuel costs. Fuel prices aren’t low by
any stretch of the imagination. However, average prices for both petrol and diesel over the September
quarter were lower than in the June quarter when prices rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle East

### Page 3

conflict. In terms of specifics, the average price for 91 unleaded was 6.5% lower in September than in
the June quarter, while diesel prices were close to 16% lower. Those moves took 0.4ppts off inflation
this quarter. 
While prices at the pump weren’t as high as they were in the June quarter, fuel costs are still painfully
high. And that’s been bleeding through into higher prices for other goods and services. Of note, the
September quarter saw large increases in passenger transport costs, with international airfares up 14%
and domestic airfares up nearly 8%. 
The September quarter also saw the annual increases in local council rates (up 6.2%) and charges for
services like water supply (up 7.5%). That continues the run of large increases seen in recent years.
Lastly, the past few months saw a 1.0% rise in food prices, largely as a result of the seasonal rise in
prices for fresh vegetables. 
On the downside, we’ve seen continued softness in housing rents, which are the largest component of
the CPI, accounting for 11.2% of the basket. With low population growth and ample supply, rents were
flat in the September quarter and have effectively been flat since the start of this year. In annual terms,
rental inflation is the lowest it’s been in more than two decades, with regions like Wellington seeing
outright falls.

Annual and core inflation 
The annual inflation rate dropped back to 3.7% in the September quarter, down from 4.1% in the year
to June and the highest level in two years.
Looking under the surface, prices in the domestically oriented non-tradables group rose 1.0% over the
quarter, less than expected. That saw annual non-tradables inflation nudging down to 3.3% (from 3.4%
in the year to June). 
Non-tradables inflation has continued to run at above-average rates over the past year. That’s despite
the softness in economic growth and the labour market. In large part, that’s due to the continued
sizeable increases in administered prices, such as electricity charges which rose 9.3% over the past
year. 
However, prices in other areas have also been firm. Non-tradables excluding housing and utilities costs
(which also omits local council rates) was 3.4% for the year. In seasonally adjusted terms, quarterly
non-tradables inflation has been tracking around 0.8% for most of the past year. It was noted that in
sectors like construction, higher materials costs are adding to prices. Feedback from business we've
heard indicates that those higher materials costs are likely related to high oil prices. 
On the imported front, lower fuel costs saw tradable prices rising by just 0.2% over the September
quarter (though above our forecast for a small fall). That saw annual tradables inflation dropping back
to 4.3%, down sharply from the 4.9% rise in the year to June. 
While fuel costs have been a key driver for tradables inflation, under the surface, other imported
inflation pressures are looking firm. Excluding food and fuel costs, tradables prices were up 2.7% over
the past year. That’s the highest level in three years. 
With big swings in fuel costs, and concern that this is spilling over into operating costs more generally,
a key focus for the RBNZ is what’s been happening to measures of core inflation (which smooth
through quarter-to-quarter swings in individual prices and instead track the underlying trend in

### Page 4

Related articles
inflation). 
On this front we did some see worrying signs. Several closely watched measures of core inflation have
picked up, and now sit at the top of (or slightly above) the RBNZ target band. In terms of specifics, we
saw: 
Reinforcing this picture of firm underlying inflation pressures, a growing number of goods have been
increasing in price. That could be an early indication that businesses, even those with a limited
exposure to fuel costs, are starting to factor in expectations of continued high inflation when setting
prices.

Outlook 
While today’s result was lower than the RBNZ assumed in their September MPS projections, the
firmness in core inflation will give the RBNZ pause for thought ahead of their policy review on 28
October. Furthermore, with a sharp rise in petrol prices in recent weeks, September’s easing in annual
inflation is likely to be just a temporary reprieve. We expect headline inflation to reaccelerate back over
4% before the end of this year, and it’s set to remain above the RBNZ’s current forecast for most of
2027. 
Market pricing and views have coalesced around an October hike which we now suspect the MPC will
see as the path of least resistance. Core inflation measures are now close to or above 3%, which will
add fuel to arguments from the MPC hawks. We will have more detail in our MPR Preview later today,
including on the implications for the shape of the tightening cycle moving forward.

CPI ex-fuel and food: 3.2% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
CPI ex-fuel, food and household energy: +2.9% yr (vs +2.5% previously)

## Document 353: India_ Degrees of rupee defence (2026-10-16)

TAKING STOCK

The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has adopted a 
multi-layered defence strategy to support the 
rupee this year.

This has entailed a combination of outright 
intervention in spot/ forwards, regulatory 
action, liquidity steps, administrative measures 
and moves to curb import -related dollar 
demand.

The rupee has appreciated by 1.8% against the 
dollar in the past five days since recent 
measures took effect, paring YTD depreciation 
to 5.5% . High oil prices, weakness in capital 
inflows, a firm dollar , tariffs/ geopolitical 
worries, energy , and risk -off sentiments have 
kept the INR under pressure since last year.

### Page 2

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

ECHOES OF 2013 WITH FEW 
DIFFERENCES

Comparisons with 2013 are apt, though 
the backdrop differs.  As for the 
catalysts, markets reacted to the US 
Fed’s taper announcement in 2013, 
which pushed US yields higher  and led 
global investors to reduce exposure to 
vulnerable EM economies. As a result, 
capital inflows weakened, exposing 
funding mismatch es for countries, 
including India, that were running wide 
current account deficits.

In 2026, markets face the spectre of 
elevated oil prices, geopolitical 
uncertainty, global competition for 
capital, a firm dollar ( DXY appreciated 
+3.8% YTD) , and US inflation/ deficits 
which have settled at levels much higher 
than pre -pandemic years. This has 
pushed up UST yields, and the US Fed 
kicked off its rate-hike cycle in Sep26.

Against this backdrop, the RBI is 
pursuing the same objective , i.e., to 
ensure rupee stability, but the nature of 
the battle differs. The defence strategy 
has shifted from ‘crisis management’ 
mode in 2013 , due to weaker domestic 
macroeconomic balances (a wide current 
account gap, high inflation, a lower 
reserve buffer, and wide fiscal deficits) , 
to a ‘terms-of-trade shock and impact of 
a firm dollar’ in 2026.

Flashpoints are  nonetheless similar, 
with reliance on portfolio financing , 
leaving the external account vulnerable 
to shifts in global risk sentiment. 
Portfolio outflows have emerged as the 
primary transmission channel through 
which external shocks have translated 
into INR depreciation pressure , 
compounded by high energy prices and a 
firm dollar.

FX PLAYBOOK

While the response framework is similar 
between 2013 and this year, the broad 
sequence and nature of macroprudential 
measures differ. This year’s multi-
layered rupee defence toolkit has 
included:

-Direct FX defence  by way of 
intervention in the spot and forwards 
market. As of Aug26, the net forward 
dollar liabilities ballooned to $200bn, 
rising by ~$63bn vs . the month before . 
Recent sell/buy FX swaps are expected 
to lower this stock in the coming months.

-Attracting dollar inflows  by way of 
special swap facilities for FCNR deposits, 
and offshore borrowings , apart from 
liberalising taxes around debt inflows,

### Page 3

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

amongst others. We discussed this here, 
here, here and here.

Taking a proactive stance, a portion of 
existing FX reserves could be earmarked 
against forward liabilities (from maturing 
FCNR deposits) , helping to mitigate 
concerns that deposit maturities or debt 
repayments could trigger a sharp
increase in dollar ⁠demand down the line.

-Domestic liquidity management. Bonds, 
to some degree, are paying the price for 
the authorities' efforts to defend the 
rupee against the additional headwind of 
weak global conditions.  INR liquidity 
surged on the back of the swap windows, 
prompting the RBI to undertake open 
market operations, VRRR auctions, sell -
buy swaps, among others. While these 
have been effective in absorbing 
balances, bond yields have drifted higher. 
10Y yields are at three-year highs and are 
expected to climb further  (DBSf: 7.40 -
7.50% by year-end).

-Moderate FX demand and speculation . 
Earlier in the year, the RBI had 
introduced change s to the banks’ net 
open position framework, and thereafter 
partially rolled back a few curbs. 
Prudential measures on FX exposures 
and hedging -related measures were 
announced in Oct26, which include d the 
introduction of a daily cash reserve with 
the RBI equal to 20% of the rupee 
equivalent of the notional value for INR -
linked derivative contracts exceeding 
$2mn used to hedge current account 
transactions, referred to as the Foreign 
Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) . 
Subsequent clarifications provided 
operational details on where the FERR 
would be maintained and that it would be 
subject to audits.

Since 12 Oct, the RBI has been providing 
dollars via designated banks to the three 
state-owned oil -marketing companies 
(Indian Oil, BPCL, and HPCL) to meet the 
their daily dollar requirements, thereby 
diverting related spot-market demand.

On hedging-related matters, the limit for 
undertaking foreign exchange derivative 
transactions without proving an 
underlying commercial exposure was 
cut to $5mn from $100mn across all ADs 
and exchange -traded currency 
derivatives. Restrictions on Rebooking , 
i.e., a uthorized dealers are prohibited 
from allowing customers to rebook 
cancelled rupee -linked derivative 
contracts (though rollovers upon 
maturity remain permitted). Lastly, 
banks must obtain written confirmation 
from users that the same underlying 
exposure has not been hedged with 
another dealer.  Data from CCI , cited by

### Page 4

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Bloomberg, pointed to importers’ 
forward dollar purchases rising 44% on 
the year to a monthly average of $64bn.

-Current account management . Efforts 
to contain structural FX outflows by way 
of higher duties and taxes on gold 
imports discourage non -essential 
purchases and production incentives to 
improve export competitiveness.

IMPACT

To assess whether the playbook has 
worked, we observe:

a) Rupee has enjoyed a brief respite. 
Despite oil prices at $105/bbl at the time 
of writing and elevated global yields, 
USDINR fell from near 96.80 to wards 
95.00, owing to measures announced in 
Oct26 following the central bank’s 25bp 
hike. Beyond temporary relief, global 
catalysts are likely to be back to the fore 
and weigh on the currency (see chart);

b) Sharp inflows under the swap 
facilities pushed the foreign reserves 
stock to a record high, providing 
policymakers sufficient buffer to 
navigate future shocks from a position of 
strength. A strong interventionist 
response has since led to a softening in 
the overall stock (see chart).

c) Macroprudential measures have led to 
distortions in other parts of the market . 
For instance, strong swap -related 
inflows had inorganically pushed 
onshore INR liquidity up sharply, 
necessitating absorption measures. 
Similarly, NOP restrictions and the more 
recent tighter requirements on hedging 
activity and counterparty transactions  
had some after -effects. Forward points 
have surged this week , with financial 
institutions pointing to relative illiquidity 
in the currency forwards market  on 
volumes and activity basis, and fewer 
avenues to deal with dollar supply.

### Page 5

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

d) Swap-related windows are a timely 
support for the country’s balance of 
payments position. From expectations of 
a third consecutive year of BOP deficit in 
FY27, the math has now swung to a 
potential surplus, even as current 
account worries remain. With energy 
prices settling north of $100/bbl, 
keeping the monthly trade deficit near 
$30bn, our FY27 current account deficit 
forecast of 1.1% of GDP faces  upside 
risks of around 0.3-0.5% of GDP. We note 
the s harp jump in ECB outflows in S ep, 
though see that as a temporary overshot 
of leverage-FCNR activity.

WHAT LIES AHEAD

Authorities are likely not done yet and 
remain ready to take further action, as 
required. RBI Governor Malhotra 
reiterated the authorities’ serious intent 
of stabilising the currency in a recent 
interview (Moneycontrol, 13 Oct), 
emphasising that “The RBI is determined 
to do whatever is necessary to ensure 
orderly movement in the foreign 
exchange market,”. He also highlighted 
that recent steps were not tantamount to 
capital control measures that were 
aimed at ensuring that forex markets 
serve genuine hedging needs rather than 
speculative bets.  Market participants 
opine that FX volumes and interest are 
likely to be subdued in a few pockets on 
the back of the recent measures, 
consequently stabilizing the rupee.

Unlike in 2013, when policy tightening 
was a part of the initial response, the 
2026 strategy has prioritised targeted 
FX and regulatory measures.  The 
sequencing suggests a deliberate effort 
to distinguish actions aimed at 
preserving currency stability from those 
intended to achieve domestic monetary 
objectives, with rates remaining a 
potential, though not immediate, tool in 
the defence framework.

However, global developments have 
altered that dynamic . Rising US yields 
and a gradual increase in the US Fed 
Funds rate (DBS baseline: a total of 75bp 
hikes) have compressed IN -US spreads. 
The recent sell -off in INR benchmark

### Page 6

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

bonds has effectively widened Gsec -
UST spreads across maturities, in 
contrast to the compression witnessed in 
regional high-yielders.

The latest announcements may not 
fully quell speculation over further 
policy action.

Upcoming measures might include -

-Markets anticipate relief on the long -
term capital gains tax structure on 
equities to draw in longer-duration 
inflows. This measure will boost inflows 
if it coincides with other factors that 
make domestic equities attractive for 
thematic investing , as global risk -free 
rates moderate.

-Street expectations also include steps 
to boost inward dollar repatriation by 
exporters to boost domestic dollar 
supply.

-Wider policy rate corridor akin to 2013.

-Policy rate response. While this might 
not be a panacea, it is a likely necessary 
condition. Raising the neutral rate might 
have mixed success in attracting capital 
market inflows ; though rate-sensitive 
flows are likely to get a fillip as investors 
seek attractive rate differentials in the 
region.

An adequate real rate cushion would 
also align with the evolving domestic 
inflation realities, which are expected to 
accelerate to 6% in the Oct-Dec quarter 
and remain above 5% well into 2027.

Given fluid global developments, we 
outline three scenarios on the rate path 
ahead. Our baseline assumption  is for 
75bp hikes in this cycle  (including Oct), 
spread between the Dece mber and 
February 2027 meetings. We don’t rule 
out an intermeeting rate hike before the 
December meeting, contingent on 
rupee movements.

Under the second scenario , s hould 
depreciation pressures on the rupee 
return, the 50bp hike might be brought 
forward to December , with another in 
February, taking cumulative hikes in this 
cycle to 100bp. The t hird scenario  is a 
more aggressive 150bp worth of hikes in 
this cycle, with 50bp front -loaded in 
December, and the rest spilling into 2027. 
Factors that  will determine whether 
these scenarios materialise are the 
resumption and scale of rupee 
depreciation, oil price direction, US Fed 
Funds rate increases (DBSf: total 75bps) 
and other risk-off events.

Path ahead

Despite these measures, we see limited 
room for sustained INR appreciation on 
grounds of manufacturing/ trade 
competitiveness. A challenging flows 
portfolio flows outlook leaves the d oor 
open to further gradual rupee 
depreciation subject to global 
developments, with high sensitivity to oil 
price movements and risk sentiments . A 
strong rate response will help introduce  
favourable rate differentials and reduce 
currency volatility.

## Document 391: Opportunities from the bond sell-off

Source PDF: all report pdf/Opportunities from the bond sell-off.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Weekly Market Views)
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

WS Global CIO Office 
2 October 2026

Opportunities from 
the bond sell-off

 The global bond sell-off is creating 
tactical and medium-term opportunities. 
In the US, September’s yield surge has 
improved the risk-reward in 10-year 
government bonds, presenting an 
attractive entry point for investors with 
a 6-12-month horizon. 
 We believe bond and money 
markets are overly hawkish about the 
Fed outlook, given disinflation is likely 
to return as the impact of oil prices and 
tariffs ease next year. 
 In equities, we stick to quality, 
especially sectors riding the AI-led 
earnings boom. The upcoming Q3 
earnings season is likely to show AI-
investments are powering sectors 
beyond the technology and 
communications services. We remain 
constructive on the semiconductor and 
memory segments.  
 We also expect the US Dollar to 
peak in the next few weeks along with 
short-to-medium-term US bond yields.
